\iota_{M}^{!}:\SI_{P}\to\Sph_{M}
sends
G
-tempered objects to
M
-tempered objects.

•

The following composition is zero:

\SI_{P}^{G\mathrm{-temp}}\to\SI_{P}\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}^{!}}\Sph_{M}\to\Sph_{M}^{M\mathrm{-atemp}}.

•

The following composition is zero:

\Sph_{M}^{M\mathrm{-atemp}}\to\Sph_{M}\xrightarrow{\iota_{M,!}^{-}}\SI_{P^{-}}\to\SI_{P^{-}}^{G\mathrm{-temp}}.

•

The functor
\iota_{M,!}^{-}:\Sph_{M}\to\SI_{P^{-}}
sends
M
-anti-tempered objects to
G
-anti-tempered objects.

In other words, (2) for
P^{-}
is equivalent to (1) for
P
. Similarly, (3) for
P^{-}
is equivalent to (4) for
P
.

Hence we just need to prove (1) and (4). Our strategy is to replace the source category
\SI_{P}
by
\Sph_{G}
. Namely, we deduce (1) and (4) from the following two results, which are proved respectively in §
3.3
and §
3.4
:

Proposition 3.2.5
.

The composition

\HL^{!}_{P}:\Sph_{G}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{U({\mathcal{K}})/U({\mathcal{O}})}}\SI_{P}\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}^{!}}\Sph_{M}

sends
G
-tempered objects to
M
-tempered objects.

Proposition 3.2.6
.

Consider the composition

\HL^{*}_{P}:\Sph_{G}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{U({\mathcal{K}})/U({\mathcal{O}})}}\SI_{P}\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}^{*}}\Sph_{M}.

An object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G}
is
G
-anti-tempered iff
\HL^{*}_{P}({\mathcal{F}})
is
M
-anti-tempered.

Remark 3.2.7
.

The notation
\HL
stands for
hyperbolic localization
because the underlying object of
\HL^{*}_{P}({\mathcal{F}})
on
\Gr_{M}
is given by the hyperbolic localization of
{\mathcal{F}}
in the literature, i.e., the object obtained by first
*
-pullback along
\Gr_{P}\to\Gr_{G}
then
!
-pushforward along
\Gr_{P}\to\Gr_{M}
. This can be seen by passing to right adjoints. Namely, when restricted to each connected component
\Gr_{M,\lambda}
of
\Gr_{M}
, both right adjoints are

\DMod(\Gr_{M,\lambda})^{M({\mathcal{O}})}\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Gr_{P,\lambda})^{M({\mathcal{O}})}\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{M({\mathcal{O}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}})}.

Remark 3.2.8
.

Theorem
3.0.1
(1) is the global analogue of Proposition
3.2.5
. We do not know any global analogue of Proposition
3.2.6
.

Remark 3.2.9
.

For the purpose of proving Theorem
3.2.1
, we only need the “only if” part of Proposition
3.2.6
. However, we think the stated stronger claim is of independent interest.

Let us continue to deduce Theorem
3.2.1
(1),(4) from the above propositions. We explain this deduction for (1), the argument for (4) is completely similar.

Since
\iota_{M,!}:\Sph_{M}\to\SI_{P}
is
\Sph_{M}
-linear, we have

\iota_{M,!}({\mathcal{G}})\simeq\Delta_{0}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}{\mathcal{G}}

for any
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Sph_{M}
, and the category
\SI_{P}
is generated under colimits by such objects. Let
\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}}
be the tempered unit of
\Sph_{G}
. Then
\SI_{P}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\subseteq\SI_{P}
is generated under colimits by

\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G}}}\Delta_{0}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}{\mathcal{G}}.

Since
\iota_{M}^{!}
is also
\Sph_{M}
-linear, we have

\iota_{M}^{!}(\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G}}}\Delta_{0}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}{\mathcal{G}})\simeq\iota_{M}^{!}(\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G}}}\Delta_{0})\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}{\mathcal{G}},

and we only need to show that the RHS is
M
-tempered. Since
\Sph_{M}^{\mathrm{temp}}\subseteq\Sph_{M}
is a two-sided ideal, we only need to show

\iota_{M}^{!}(\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G}}}\Delta_{0})

is
M
-tempered. But this object is
\HL_{P}^{!}(\mathbf{1}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{\Sph_{G}})
, which is
M
-tempered by Proposition
3.2.5
.

∎[Theorem
3.2.1
]

3.3.
Hyperbolic localizations and temperedness: I

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
3.2.5
, which states that the functor
\HL^{!}_{P}:\Sph_{G}\to\Sph_{M}
preserves temperedness.

As mentioned in the beginning of this section, we need to use the following result of Lafforgue:

Theorem 3.3.1

(​​
[
Laf09
]
, see also
[
Ber21c
, §3.1]
)
.

Fix an embedding
{\mathcal{O}}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and identify
\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
with
G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})
. Then we have a pair of inverse functors:

\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}:\Sph_{G}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})):\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/G}.

More precisely,
\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/G}
is the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}})}=:\Sph_{G}

and
\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}
is defined similarly.

Note that both functors in the theorem are
\Sph_{G}
-linear by Appendix
A
.

We first deduce Proposition
3.2.5
from the following result
4
4

4

This result seems to be well-known to experts. We provide a proof at the end of this subsection because we cannot find one in the literature.
.

Proposition 3.3.2
.

We have a canonical commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Sph_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}}
\scriptstyle{\HL_{P}^{!}}
\textstyle{\Sph_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\CT_{P,*}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})).}

Warning 3.3.3
.

The functor
\HL_{P}^{!}
factors through
\SI_{P}
and
\CT_{P,*}
factors through
\DMod(\Bun_{P}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))
, but the category
\SI_{P}
is
not
equivalent to
\DMod(\Bun_{P}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))
.

Proof of Proposition
3.2.5
.

By Proposition
3.3.2
, we only need to show when
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, the functor
\CT_{P,*}
sends
G
-tempered objects to
M
-tempered objects.

The full subcategory
\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))^{G\mathrm{-temp}}
can be explicitly described as follows.

For any curve
X
, let
\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}}\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G})
to be the full subcategory generated under colimits by objects of the form
j_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
, where
j:U\to\Bun_{G}
is a
quasi-compact
open substack of
\Bun_{G}
and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\DMod(U)
. Then
[
Ber21b
, Theorem 3.5.1]
says

\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))^{\mathrm{temp}}\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))^{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}

as full subcategories of
\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))
.

On the other hand, using the fact that
p:\Bun_{P}\to\Bun_{G}
is quasi-compact when restricted to each connected component of the source, we see that
\CT_{P,*}
sends
\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
into
\DMod(\Bun_{M})^{{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
. Hence, when
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, the functor
\CT_{P,*}
preserves tempered objects by the last paragraph.

∎[Proposition
3.2.5
]

Before proving Proposition
3.3.2
, let us mention the following corollary:

Corollary 3.3.4
.

We have a canonical commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Sph_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/P({\mathcal{O}})}[2\dim(G/P)]}
\textstyle{\Sph_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{M,!}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\Eis_{P,!}}

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
3.3.2
and Corollary
3.1.8
.

∎[Corollary
3.3.4
]

We prove Proposition
3.3.2
in the rest of this subsection. In spite of Warning
3.3.3
, we want to relate
\CT_{*}
to
\SI_{P}
. This is achieved in the following construction, which works for any curve
X
.

Proposition-Construction 3.3.5
.

There are canonical
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}:\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G}):\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}

such that the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{M})\simeq\Sph_{M,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G})

is equivalent to
(\Eis_{P,!},\CT_{P,*})
.

Remark 3.3.6
.

The functors
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
and
\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
were introduced by D. Gaitsgory using the Drinfeld compactification. Unfortunately, his construction is only documented in the case
P=B
in unpublished notes (see
[
ABC+
, Talk Ja-4]
). Our construction below is different from his original one.

Proof.

Consider the
M({\mathcal{O}}_{x})
-torsor
\Bun_{M}^{\mathrm{level}_{\infty x}}
on
\Bun_{M}
that classifies
M
-torsors on
X
equipped with a trivialization over
\Spec({\mathcal{O}}_{x})
. Define

\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}:=\Gr_{G,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M({\mathcal{O}}_{x})}}\Bun_{M}^{\mathrm{level}_{\infty x}}:

this space classifies triples
({\mathcal{F}}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{M},\alpha)
where
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}
(resp.
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}
) is a
G
-torsor (resp.
M
-torsor) on
X
and
\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}_{G}|_{X\setminus x}\to G\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{X\setminus x}
is an isomorphism of
G
-torsors on
X\setminus x
. We have a map

\pi:\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\to\Bun_{G}

that remembers only the
G
-torsor
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}
. Let
d:=\mathrm{dim.rel.}(\Bun_{P},\Bun_{M})
be the locally constant function on
\Bun_{M}
whose values are the dimensions of fibers of
\Bun_{P}\to\Bun_{M}
. The functor
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
is defined to be the (unique) colimit-preserving functor such that

(12)

\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}({\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}{\mathcal{M}}):=\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})[-2d],

for any
{\mathcal{F}}\in\SI_{P,x}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{M})
. To justify well-definedness, we need to check:

(a)

\pi_{!}
is well-defined on
{\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}
;

(b)

For any
{\mathcal{K}}\in\Sph_{M,x}
, we have canonical equivalence

\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}(({\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}{\mathcal{K}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}({\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}({\mathcal{K}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}{\mathcal{M}})).

We first prove (a). Since
\SI_{P,x}
is generated under colimits by the image of
\iota_{M,x,!}
, we can assume
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\iota_{M,x,!}({\mathcal{G}})
for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Sph_{M,x}
. Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\Gr_{P,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}}
\textstyle{\Bun_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\Bun_{M}.}

A diagram chase shows

\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ\rho_{!}\circ v^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}).

Consider the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{P}\times_{\Bun_{M}}(\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{M}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

By Lemma
3.3.7
below, the map

\theta:\Gr_{P,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\to\Bun_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M}}}(\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M})

is universally homologically contractible. In particular,
\theta^{!}
is fully faithful and hence
\theta_{!}\circ\theta^{!}\simeq\Id
.
It follows that

\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ\rho_{!}\circ v^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ(\rho^{\prime})_{!}\circ\theta_{!}\circ\theta^{!}\circ{q^{\prime}}^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ(\rho^{\prime})_{!}\circ{q^{\prime}}^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}).

Since
\Bun_{P}\to\Bun_{M}
is smooth, the base-change isomorphism yields

\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ(\rho^{\prime})_{!}\circ{q^{\prime}}^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})\simeq p_{!}\circ q^{!}\circ\sigma_{!}({\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}).

Note that the RHS is well-defined because
\sigma
is ind-proper and
p_{!}\circ q^{!}\simeq p_{!}\circ q^{*}[2d]\simeq\Eis_{P,!}[2d]
is well-defined. This proves (a), as well as the last claim in the proposition (modulo claim (b)). Moreover, note that if
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}
are compact, then so is
\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})
.

Now (b) follows from the fact that the diagonal
M({\mathcal{K}}_{x})
-action on
\Gr_{G,x}\times\Bun_{M}^{\mathrm{level}_{\infty x}}
stabilizes the fibers of
\pi
.

By the previous discussion, the functor
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
preserves compact objects, hence its right adjoint
\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
is continuous. Also,
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
has a natural
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear structure because
\pi
is
G({\mathcal{K}}_{x})
-equivariant. Then Appendix
A
guarantees that
\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
is strictly
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear.

∎[Proposition-Construction
3.3.5
]

The following lemma was used above.

Lemma 3.3.7
.

The map

\theta:\Gr_{P,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\to\Bun_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M}}}(\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M})

induced by the obvious map
\Gr_{P,x}\to\Bun_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M}}}\Gr_{M,x}
is universally homologically contractible.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
2.4.2
.

For any finite type affine test scheme
s:S\to\Bun_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M}}}(\Gr_{M,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M})
, we need to show the base-change
\theta^{\prime}:S^{\prime}\to S
of
\theta
along
s
is homologically contractible. By definition,
s
corresponds to
({\mathcal{F}}_{P},{\mathcal{F}}_{M},\alpha)
where
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}
(resp.
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}
) is a
P
-torsor (resp.
M
-torsor) on
X\times S
and

\alpha:M\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{P}}{\mathcal{F}}_{P}|_{(X\setminus x)\times S}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{(X\setminus x)\times S}

is an isomorphism of
M
-torsors on
(X\setminus x)\times S
. Hence, for any finite type affine test
S
-scheme
T
, the groupoid (which is actually a set)
\Maps_{S}(T,S^{\prime})
classifies isomorphisms

\beta:{\mathcal{F}}_{P}|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}\to P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}

of
P
-torsors on
(X\setminus x)\times T
such that
M\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{P}}\beta
is equal to the composition of
\alpha|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}
with
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}\simeq M\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{P}}(P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{M})|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}
. It follows that
\Maps_{S}(T,S^{\prime})
is the set of
P
-equivariant maps
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}|_{(X\setminus x)\times T}\to P/M
. Since
(X\setminus x)\times T
is affine, any
P
-torsor on it has an
M
-structure. Hence
\Maps_{S}(T,S^{\prime})
is a non-empty torsor for the group
\Maps((X\setminus x)\times T,P/M)
. Since
P/M
is an affine space and
X\setminus x
is affine, the functor
T\mapsto\Maps((X\setminus x)\times T,P/M)
is represented by
S\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty}
, where
{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty}
is the infinite dimensional affine space associated to the vector space
\Gamma(X\setminus x,{\mathcal{O}})^{\oplus\dim(P/M)}
. So we have a free and transitive action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty}
on the fibers of
\theta^{\prime}:S^{\prime}\to S
, which implies
\theta^{\prime}
is homologically contractible.

∎[Lemma
3.3.7
]

We also need the following lemma:

Lemma 3.3.8
.

We have a pair of inverse functors:

\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}:\SI_{P}\adjoint\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}:\Av_{*}^{M({\mathcal{O}})/M}.

More precisely,
\Av_{*}^{M({\mathcal{O}})/M}
is the composition

\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}})}=:\SI_{P}

and
\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}
is defined similarly.

Proof.

We have adjoint pairs similar to

\iota_{M,!}:\DMod(\Gr_{M})^{M({\mathcal{O}})}\adjoint\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}})}:\iota_{M}^{!},

but with
M({\mathcal{O}})
-invariance replaced by
M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})
-invariance (resp.
M
-invariance). We abuse notation by writing these adjoint pairs as
(\iota_{M,!},\iota_{M}^{!})
. By construction, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{M}^{!}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{M}^{!}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{M}^{!}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{M})^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oblv}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{M})^{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{M})^{M({\mathcal{O}})}}

that is left adjointable along vertical directions. Indeed, the left adjointability follows from the fact that
\iota_{M,!}:\DMod(\Gr_{M})\to\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})}
is
M({\mathcal{K}})
-linear. Recall the composition of the bottom row is an equivalence: it is just Lafforgue’s equivalence for the reductive group
M
. Hence the composition of the top row is also an equivalence by the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem on monadicity.

∎[Lemma
3.3.8
]

Proof of Proposition
3.3.2
.

By Proposition-Construction
3.3.5
, we only need to construct the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Sph_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{U({\mathcal{K}})/U({\mathcal{O}})}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P}^{!}}
\textstyle{\Sph_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\CT_{P,*,\mathrm{ext}}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{M}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})).}

The right square is obvious. For the left one, recall
\Eis_{P,!,\mathrm{ext}}
is defined by the formula

\Eis_{P,!,\mathrm{ext}}({\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}{\mathcal{M}}):=\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}),

where
{\mathcal{F}}\in\SI_{P}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{M}/M({\mathcal{O}}^{-}))
, so that

{\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}\in\DMod(\Gr_{G}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{G}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M({\mathcal{O}})}}M({\mathcal{K}}))^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}.

Via the above equivalence,
\pi_{!}
is the partially defined functor

\DMod(\Gr_{G}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M({\mathcal{O}})}}M({\mathcal{K}}))^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{push}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})).

Since
{\mathcal{F}}
is
U({\mathcal{K}})
-equivariant, the image of
{\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}}
under the first functor in the above composition is
U({\mathcal{K}})
-equivariant. In particular,
\Eis_{P,!,\mathrm{ext}}
factors as

\SI_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\xrightarrow{c}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/P({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})),

where
\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/P({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}
is the partially defined functor

\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{P({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})).

It is easy to check the composition

\SI_{P}\simeq\SI_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\xrightarrow{c}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}

is equivalent to the functor
\Av_{!}^{M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/M}
in Lemma
3.3.8
. In particular,
c
is an equivalence and therefore
\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/P({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}
is well-defined. It follows
\CT_{P,*,\mathrm{ext}}
is equivalent to

\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{U(K)/U({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{c^{-1}}\SI_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})).

Hence, it remains to check that the following diagram is naturally commutative:

\textstyle{\Sph_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{!}^{U({\mathcal{K}})/U({\mathcal{O}})}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}^{U(K)/U({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}^{M({\mathcal{O}})/M}}

It is easy to check that the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{U(K)/U({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{U({\mathcal{K}})M({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{M({\mathcal{O}})/M}}\SI_{P}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{Ph_{P}/P({\mathcal{O}})}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{Ph_{P}}

is equivalent to

\Av_{*}^{Ph_{P}/P}:\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{P}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{Ph_{P}}.

Hence, by Lemma
3.1.6
and Lemma
3.1.7
, we only need to show that

\Sph_{G}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{Ph_{P}/P}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{Ph_{P}}

is equivalent to the forgetful functor. Since
G(O)/G\simeq Ph_{P}/P
, the functor
\Av_{*}^{Ph_{P}/P}
factors as

\DMod(\Bun_{G}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}))\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/G}}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{Ph_{P}}.

Then we are done because
\Av_{!}^{G({\mathcal{O}}^{-})/G}
and
\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/G}
are inverse to each other.

∎[Proposition
3.3.2
]

3.4.
Hyperbolic localizations and temperedness: II

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
3.2.6
, which says that the functor
\HL^{*}_{P}:\Sph_{G}\to\Sph_{M}
detects anti-temperedness.

Consider the Borel subgroup
B\cap M
of
M
and the corresponding functor
\HL^{*}_{B_{M}}:\Sph_{M}\to\Sph_{T}
. We claim
\HL^{*}_{B}\simeq\HL^{*}_{B_{M}}\circ\HL^{*}_{P}
. Indeed, this follows from Remark
3.2.7
, the base-change isomorphism, and the identification
\Gr_{B_{M}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Gr_{M}}}\Gr_{P}\simeq\Gr_{B}
.

Hence, to prove the proposition, we can assume
P=B
is the Borel subgroup. Recall that any object in
\Sph_{T}
is
T
-tempered. Hence we only need to show that an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G}
is
G
-anti-tempered iff
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq 0
. Equivalently, by Lemma
2.1.3
, we only need to show that an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G}
has vanishing cohomologies (with respect to the perverse t-structure) iff
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq 0
.

Consider the locally constant function
d
on
\Gr_{T}
whose value on the connected component
\Gr_{T,\lambda}
is
\langle 2\check{\rho},\lambda\rangle
. It is well-known (see e.g.
[
MV07
]
) that for
{\mathcal{M}}\in\Sph_{G}^{\heartsuit}
, the object
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{M}})[d]
is contained in
\Sph_{T}^{\heartsuit}
, and that the resulting functor
\Sph_{G}^{\heartsuit}\to\Sph_{T}^{\heartsuit}
can be identified with the forgetful functor
\Rep(\check{G})\to\Rep(\check{T})
.

We claim this formally implies that the functor
\HL^{*}_{B}(-)[d]:\Sph_{G}\to\Sph_{T}
is right t-exact. To prove the claim, recall that any compact object in
\Sph_{G}
has bounded cohomologies and that the perverse t-structure on
\Sph_{G}
is compatible with filtered colimits. Now, any object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G}^{\leq 0}
can be written as a filtered colimit
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{i}\,}F_{i}
with
F_{i}
compact. Then
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\tau^{\leq 0}{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{i}\,}\tau^{\leq 0}F_{i}
. Since
\tau^{\leq 0}F_{i}
has bounded cohomologies, it is a finite colimit of
H^{-j}(\tau^{\leq 0}F_{i})[j]
for
j\geq 0
. Hence the functor
\HL^{*}_{B}(-)[d]
sends each
\tau^{\leq 0}F_{i}
to a finite colimit of objects in
\Sph_{T}^{\leq 0}
. It follows that
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{F}})[d]\in\Sph_{T}^{\leq 0}
as desired. This proves the claim.

Combining the claim with the fact that the functor
\Sph_{G}^{\heartsuit}\to\Sph_{T}^{\heartsuit}
is conservative, we see that
{\mathcal{F}}
has vanishing cohomologies iff
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{F}})
does. But the latter condition is equivalent to
\HL^{*}_{B}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq 0
because
\Sph_{T}^{\leq-\infty}
is the zero category.

∎[Proposition
3.2.6
]

3.5.
Proof of Theorem
3.0.2
: a local-to-global argument

In this subsection, we deduce Theorem
3.0.2
from Theorem
3.2.1
. The key ingredient is the following construction.

Proposition-Construction 3.5.1
.

There are canonical
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}:\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,P):\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}

such that the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{M})\simeq\Sph_{M,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)

is equivalent to
(\iota_{M,!},\iota_{M}^{!})
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition-Construction
3.3.5
, where we defined
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}:\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G}):\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}

using
!
-pushforward along the map
\pi:\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\to\Bun_{G}
. By definition, the map
\pi
factors as

\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}\to\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}.

Hence
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
factors through as

\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}).

Consider the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

As in the proof of Proposition-Construction
3.3.5
, one can show that this functor is equivalent to

\DMod(\Bun_{M})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

Hence, by Lemma
2.3.7
, it factors through

\DMod(\Bun_{M})\xrightarrow{\iota_{M,!}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P).

We thus have shown that
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}
factors through a functor

\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P),

which is the desired functor
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. We let
\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
be its continuous right adjoint.

∎[Proposition-Construction
3.5.1
]

Using Proposition-Construction
3.5.1
, Theorem
3.0.2
(1) and (2) obviously follow from Theorem
3.2.1
(1) and (2).

To proceed, we need one more geometric input. We claim we have the following diagram which is Cartesian at least at the reduced level:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{P,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Namely, unwinding the definitions, the desired fiber product classifies triples consisting of:

(i)

an
M
-torsor
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}
on
X
;

(ii)

a
P
-torsor
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}
on
X
;

(iii)

an isomorphism
\alpha:G\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{P}}{\mathcal{F}}_{P}|_{X\setminus x}\to G\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{X\setminus x}
between
G
-torsors on
X\setminus x

with the following property: there exists an open subset
U\subseteq X
such that
\alpha
induces an isomorphism
P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{P}}{\mathcal{F}}_{P}|_{U}\simeq P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{M}|_{U}
of
P
-torsors on
U
. Since
G/P
is separated, this last equivalence can be uniquely extended over
X\setminus x
. Meanwhile, such extended isomorphism also determines
\alpha
. Hence the above data are exactly classified by
\Gr_{P,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M}
. This proves the claim.

Using the base-change isomorphism for the above Cartesian square, up to a cohomological shift (inherited by that in (
12
)), the functor

(13)

\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\xrightarrow{\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}^{*}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})

is equivalent to

(14)

\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}^{*}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}\Sph_{M,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{M}).

We also have a similar right adjoint version of this equivalence.

Now one can again deduce Theorem
3.0.2
(3)(4) from Theorem
3.2.1
(3)(4). We explain this for (3) and leave (4) to the readers. Note that the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}\DMod(\Bun_{M})

is conservative because it is a factor of the conservative functor
\iota_{M}^{!}
. Hence an object in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
is
G
-tempered (resp.
G
-anti-tempered) iff it is sent to a
G
-tempered object (resp.
G
-anti-tempered) by the functor
\CT_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. Passing to the left adjoints, we see that
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{\mathrm{temp}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{\mathrm{atemp}}
) is generated under colimits by images of
G
-tempered objects (resp.
G
-anti-tempered objects) under
\Eis_{P,\mathrm{ext},x}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. Hence, to prove Theorem
3.0.2
(4), we only need to show that the composition (
13
) sends
G
-anti-tempered objects to
M
-anti-tempered objects. By the last paragraph, we only need to prove this for (
14
). But this follows immediately from Theorem
3.2.1
(4).

∎[Theorem
3.0.2
]

3.6.
A (conjectural) spectral explanation of the local statement

Assuming certain conjectures (which are actually undocumented known results), Theorem
3.0.2
can also be proved by translating to the spectral side. For completeness, we provide the argument in this remark but assume the readers are familiar with some works on the subject, and in particular with the theory of singular support as in
[
AG15
]
.

The main theorem of
[
ABG04
]
provides an equivalence

\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}_{\mathrm{loc.c}}\simeq\Coh((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{n}})/{\check{B}}),

where
I:=G({\mathcal{O}})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{G}}B
is the Iwahori subgroup and
\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}_{\mathrm{loc.c}}\subseteq\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}
is the full subcategory of
locally compact objects
, i.e., those objects whose image in
\DMod(\Gr_{G})
are compact. Therefore

\Ind(\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}_{\mathrm{loc.c}})\simeq\IndCoh((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{n}})/{\check{B}}).

The category
\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}
can be obtained from the LHS by a
renormalization
construction, which corresponds to a modification of
singular supports
on the RHS (see
[
AG15
, §12]
for a similar construction for
\Sph_{G}
). More precisely, we have

\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}\simeq\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{b}})/\check{B}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{n}})/{\check{B}}),

where
5
5

5

In this formula, we fix a
\check{G}
-equivariant self-duality
\check{\mathfrak{g}}\simeq\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
.

\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{b}})/\check{B}\subseteq\check{\mathfrak{b}}/\check{B}\simeq\Sing((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{n}})/{\check{B}})
. By Raskin’s equivalence
\SI_{B}\simeq\DMod(\Gr_{G})^{I}
(see
[
Ras16
, Corollary 6.2.3]
), we obtain a spectral description for
\SI_{B}
. This description can be generalized to any parabolic subgroups
6
6

6

During the preparation of this paper, J. Campbell and S. Raskin announced a new proof of the derived Satake equivalence using chiral algebras and their method can also prove this conjecture.

P
:

Conjecture 3.6.1
.

For any standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, let
\check{P}
be the corresponding parabolic subgroup of
\check{G}
. Then there is a canonical equivalence

\SI_{P}\simeq\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{p}})/\check{P}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}})\simeq\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{p}})/\check{P}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}),

where
\check{\mathfrak{u}}
is the Lie algebra of the unipotent radical of
\check{P}
and
\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{p}})/\check{P}\subseteq\check{\mathfrak{p}}/\check{P}\simeq\Sing((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}})
. This equivalence is required to satisfy the following conditions:

•

It is compatible with the actions of the geometric Satake
monoidal
equivalences

\Sph_{G}\simeq\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{g}})/\check{G}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\pt)/{\check{G}}),\;\Sph_{M}\simeq\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{m}})/\check{M}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{M}});

•

The standard object
\Delta_{0}\in\SI_{P}
corresponds to the image of the trivial representation under the functor

\Rep(\check{P})\simeq\IndCoh(\pt/\check{P})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{push}}\IndCoh((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}})\xrightarrow{\Psi}\IndCoh_{\Nilp(\check{\mathfrak{p}})/\check{P}}((\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}}).

Using the convolution actions on
\Delta_{0}
, it is easy to see

Corollary 3.6.2
.

Assuming Conjecture
3.6.1
, we have

(1)

The functor
\iota_{M,!}:\Sph_{M}\to\SI_{P}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram
7
7

7

We always mean
!
-pullback along the right arm and then
*
-pushforward along the left arm. Part of the claim says such
!
-pull-
*
-push functors send objects with given singular supports to objects with desired singular supports.

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}

(2)

Up to tensoring with a line bundle on
(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}
, the functor
\iota_{M}^{!}:\SI_{P}\to\Sph_{M}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}};

(3)

The functor
\iota_{M}^{*}:\SI_{P}\to\Sph_{M}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}}}\pt)/{\check{M}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}}

(4)

Up to tensoring with a line bundle on
(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}
, the functor
\iota_{M,*}:\Sph_{M}\to\SI_{P}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{u}})/{\check{P}}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}}}\pt)/{\check{M}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/\check{M}

(5)

The functor
\Av_{!}^{U({\mathcal{K}})/U({\mathcal{O}})}:\Sph_{G}\to\SI_{P}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\pt)/\check{G};

(6)

The functor
\Av_{*}^{G({\mathcal{O}})/P({\mathcal{O}})}:\SI_{P}\to\Sph_{G}
corresponds to
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following diagram

(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\pt)/\check{G}\leftarrow(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}\to(\pt\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\check{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\pt)/{\check{P}}.

Then one can deduce Theorem
3.2.1
from the above result by calculating the singular supports using singular differentials.

4.
The building blocks
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)

In this section, we define and study the building blocks
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
of the automorphic gluing category. We fix a closed point
x\in X
and an identification
{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}
. Definitions in this section are supposed to be independent of such choices
8
8

8

We will prove this in a future paper.
.

In §
4.1
, we extend the functorialities of the parabolic categories
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
in such a way that the functors
\iota_{M,!}:\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
\Eis^{\mathrm{enh}}_{Q\to P}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
are instances of the same construction. These new functorialities will be used in the definition of
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
.

In §
4.2
, for an inclusion of parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P
of
G
, we define the notion of
P
-tempered objects in the parabolic category
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
, and define
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
as the full subcategory of these
P
-tempered objects. We study their functorial properties: these are the main ingredient for the construction of the automorphic gluing category. There are two kinds of functorialities and they are adjoint to each other: for the left adjoint one,
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
is covariant in
Q
and
G
, but contravariant in
P
.

In §
4.3
-
4.6
, we provide proofs for results in §
4.2
. We suggest that the readers skip them first and proceed to §
5
, where we use the building blocks
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
to state our main theorem, the
automorphic gluing theorem
.

Remark 4.0.1
.

The letter
{\mathcal{W}}
in
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
is due to its close relation with the extended Whittaker categories defined in
[
Gai15b
]
. This relation has already been indicated in
[
Ber21b
, Theorem 1.4.8]
and in the introduction. We do not further explore this topic in this paper.

4.1.
More functorialities of
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)

In this subsection, we extend the functorialities of the parabolic categories
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
.

Notation 4.1.1
.

For any parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq R
of
G
, write

{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q):={\mathcal{I}}(R/U_{R},Q/U_{R}),

where
R/U_{R}
is the Levi quotient group of
R
and
Q/U_{R}
is the image of
Q
under the map
R\to R/U_{R}
.

For any parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq R
of
G
, consider the diagram

\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\xleftarrow{p^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}}\Bun_{R}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\xrightarrow{q^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}}\Bun_{R/U_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

We have:

Lemma 4.1.2
.

The map
q^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
is relatively represented by quasi-compact smooth algebraic stacks. Also, the functor
(p^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})_{!}\circ(q^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{*}
is well-defined on
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
and sends it into
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
.

Proof.

It is easy to see the canonical map
{\mathbb{B}}Q\to{\mathbb{B}}(Q/U_{R})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}(R/U_{R})}}{\mathbb{B}}R
is an isomorphism. Hence it induces an isomorphism

\Bun_{R}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{R/U_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{R/U_{R}}}}\Bun_{R}.

Then the first claim follows from the fact that
q:\Bun_{R}\to\Bun_{R/U_{R}}
is relatively represented by quasi-compact smooth algebraic stacks.

For the second claim, by Lemma
2.3.7
(applied to
{\mathcal{I}}(R/U_{R},Q/U_{R})
), we can pre-compose with the functor
\iota_{M_{Q},!}:\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
. Using the base-change isomorphism, this composition is given by
*
-pull-
!
-push along the diagram

\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{Q/U_{R}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{R/U_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{R}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{M_{Q}}.

Again, the isomorphism
{\mathbb{B}}Q\to{\mathbb{B}}(Q/U_{R})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}(R/U_{R})}}{\mathbb{B}}R
induces an isomorphism

\Bun_{Q}\to\Bun_{Q/U_{R}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{R/U_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{R}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Hence, it remains to show that the
*
-pull-
!
-push along the diagram

\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{Q}\to\Bun_{M_{Q}}

is well-defined and has image in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
. But this is just Lemma
2.3.7
.

∎[Lemma
4.1.2
]

Definition 4.1.3
.

For any inclusion of parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq R
of
G
, Lemma
4.1.2
provides adjoint functors

\Eis_{R,!}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q):\CT_{R,*}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Note that the right adjoint
\CT_{R,*}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
is continuous because
\Eis_{R,!}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
preserves compact objects.

We have the following analogue of Theorem
2.2.3
:

Proposition 4.1.4

(2nd adjointness for
\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
)
.

For any
standard
parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P
of
G
, let
P^{-}
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
opposite to
P
and
M_{P}:=P\cap P^{-}
be the Levi
subgroup
. Then the functor
\CT_{P,*}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
is canonically equivalent to
*
-pull-
!
-push along the diagram

\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Proof.

We only need to prove that the functor in the statement is right adjoint to
\Eis_{P,!}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. When
Q=P
, this is just
[
Che20a
, Theorem 1.3.1]
. The proof there also works in the general case. Namely, let
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to T
be a group homomorphism such that the adjoint
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
G
has attractor, repeller and fixed loci given by
P,P^{-}
and
M_{P}
. The restricted action on
Q
has attractor, repeller and fixed loci given by
Q,Q\cap P^{-}
and
Q\cap M_{P}
. Note that we have
Q\cap P^{-}=Q\cap M_{P}
because
Q\subseteq P
. Hence, as in
loc.cit.
, we can construct a Drinfeld input (see
[
Che20a
, §3.2-3.3]
and
[
Dri13
, Appendix C]
) with the two correspondences are given by

\displaystyle\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

\displaystyle\leftarrow

\displaystyle\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}};

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}

\displaystyle\leftarrow

\displaystyle\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Then we are done by applying Drinfeld’s theorem on adjunctions (see
[
Che20a
, Theorem 3.2.8]
and
[
Dri13
, Appendix C]
).

∎[Proposition
4.1.4
]

Proposition-Construction 4.1.5
.

Let
\Arr(\Par_{G})
be the category of arrows of parabolic subgroups of
G
. There is a canonical functor

{\mathcal{I}}(-,-)_{\Eis}:\Arr(\Par_{G})\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)

such that

•

For fixed
Q
, a morphism
[R_{1}\supseteq Q]\to[R_{2}\supseteq Q]
is sent to the functor

\Eis_{R_{1}/U_{R_{2}},!}^{Q/U_{R_{2}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}:{\mathcal{I}}(R_{1},Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q);

•

For fixed
R
, a morphism
[R\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R\supseteq Q_{2}]
is sent to the functor

\Eis_{Q_{1}/U_{R}\to Q_{2}/U_{R}}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2}).

In particular, we obtain a functor

{\mathcal{I}}(-,-)_{\CT}:\Arr(\Par_{G})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)

by passing to right adjoints.

Proof.

By definition, we only need to construct a functor

\Arr(\Par_{G})\to\Corr(\PreStk),\;[R\supseteq Q]\mapsto\Bun_{R/U_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

such that a morphism
[R_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
is sent to the correspondence

\Bun_{R_{1}/U_{R_{1}}}^{Q_{1}/U_{R_{1}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{R_{1}/U_{R_{2}}}^{Q_{1}/U_{R_{2}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{R_{2}/U_{R_{2}}}^{Q_{2}/U_{R_{2}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

To do this, it suffices to check that composition of morphisms is well-defined, i.e., for a chain
[R_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]\to[R_{3}\supseteq Q_{3}]
, we need the canonical map

\Bun_{R_{1}/U_{R_{3}}}^{Q_{1}/U_{R_{3}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{R_{1}/U_{R_{2}}}^{Q_{1}/U_{R_{2}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{R_{2}/U_{R_{2}}}^{Q_{2}/U_{R_{2}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{R_{2}/U_{R_{3}}}^{Q_{2}/U_{R_{3}}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

to be an isomorphism. But this follows from the canonical isomorphisms

{\mathbb{B}}(R_{1}/U_{R_{3}})\to{\mathbb{B}}(R_{1}/U_{R_{2}})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}(R_{2}/U_{R_{2}})}}{\mathbb{B}}(R_{2}/U_{R_{3}}),\;{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{1}/U_{R_{3}})\to{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{1}/U_{R_{2}})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{2}/U_{R_{2}})}}{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{2}/U_{R_{3}}).

∎[Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
]

Notation 4.1.6
.

For a morphism
[R_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Arr(\Par_{G})
, we write

\Eis_{[R_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]}:{\mathcal{I}}(R_{1},Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q_{2}):\CT_{[R_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]\leftarrow[R_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]}

for the adjoint functors provided by Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
. We omit the subscripts if there is no ambiguity.

4.2.
Tempered objects in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)

In this subsection, we study various temperedness conditions in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
and use them to define the building blocks
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

For any parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, consider the
\Sph_{G}
-action on
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
given by Proposition
2.3.15
and the identification
{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}
. Let
{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq P)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
be the full subcategory of
G
-tempered objects, i.e.,

{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq P):={\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}.

Definition 4.2.2
.

For parabolic subgroups
P\subseteq R
of
G
, we write

{\mathcal{W}}(R,R\supseteq P):={\mathcal{W}}(R/U_{R},R/U_{R}\supseteq P/U_{R}).

This is a full subcategory of
{\mathcal{I}}(R,P)={\mathcal{I}}(R/U_{R},P/U_{R})
. Objects in
{\mathcal{I}}(R,P)
are said to be
R
-tempered
if they are contained in
{\mathcal{W}}(R,R\supseteq P)
.

Definition 4.2.3
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
is said to be
P
-tempered
if its image under the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
is
P
-tempered. Let
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
be the full subcategory of
P
-tempered objects, i.e., it is defined by the following Cartesian diagram

(15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CT}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CT}

We also write
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P):={\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq P)
.

Example 4.2.4
.

We have
{\mathcal{W}}(R,B\supseteq B)={\mathcal{I}}(R,B)
because of Example
2.1.7
.

Warning 4.2.5
.

For
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
, there is
no

\Sph_{M_{P}}
-action on the category
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
unless
P=R
, hence we can
not
define the notion of
M_{P}
-tempered objects in this category using the theory in §
2.1
. This is the reason we use “
P
-tempered” rather than “
M_{P}
-tempered” in the above definition.

To describe the functorialities of
{\mathcal{W}}(-,-\supseteq-)
, we need the following category:

Definition 4.2.6
.

Let
\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})
be the category of twisted chains
[R\supseteq P\supseteq Q]
of parabolic subgroups of
G
, i.e., the objects of
\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})
are standard parabolic subgroups
[R\supseteq P\supseteq Q]
, and there is a unique morphism from
[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[R_{2}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
iff we have following commutative diagram

\textstyle{R_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{P_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\supseteq}
\textstyle{Q_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\supseteq}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{R_{2}}
\textstyle{P_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\supseteq}
\textstyle{Q_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\supseteq}

We prove the following proposition in §
4.5
.

Theorem 4.2.7
.

For any morphism from
[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[R_{2}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})
, the functor

\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(R_{1},Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q_{2})

restricts to a functor

\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}).

In particular, the functor (see Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
)

{\mathcal{I}}(-,-)_{\Eis}:\Arr(\Par_{G})\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)

induces a functor

{\mathcal{W}}(-,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}:\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq P\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q).

The following two results logically follow from Theorem
4.2.7
. But in fact the proof of the theorem uses them. We prove them in §
4.3
and §
4.4
.

Proposition 4.2.8

(Case
R_{1}=P_{1}=P_{2}=P,Q_{1}=Q_{2}=Q,R_{2}=R
)
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, the functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
preserves
P
-tempered objects. In other words, it induces a functor

\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q).

Remark 4.2.9
.

Because of the lack of
\Sph_{M_{P}}
-action on
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
, the above proposition is
non-obvious
.

Proposition 4.2.10

(Case
R_{1}=R_{2}=R,Q_{1}=Q_{2}=Q
)
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P_{1}\subseteq P_{2}\subseteq R
of
G
, any
P_{2}
-tempered object in
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
is
P_{1}
-tempered, i.e.,
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P_{2}\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}(R,P_{1}\supseteq Q)
.

Remark 4.2.11
.

When
Q=P_{1},P_{2}=R=G
, the above proposition can be viewed as a global analogue of Corollary
3.2.2
.

The proof of the following corollary is independent of Theorem
4.2.7
. In fact, the proof of the theorem uses it.

Corollary 4.2.12
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, the category
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)
is compactly generated.

Proof.

The functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)
in Proposition
4.2.8
preserves compact objects and its image generates the target under colimits because its right adjoint is continuous and conservative. Hence, we just need to show
{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)
is compactly generated. Replacing
G
by
P/U_{P}
, we can assume
P=G
. In other words, it remains to show that
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)^{G\mathrm{-temp}}
is compactly generated: this follows from Lemma
2.3.7
and Lemma
2.1.9
.

∎[Corollary
4.2.12
]

Corollary 4.2.13
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, the inclusion functor
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
has a continuous right adjoint

\mathrm{temp}_{P}:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q).

Furthermore, the commutative diagram (
15
) induces a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CT}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CT}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{temp}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{temp}_{P}}

Proof.

To prove the first claim, we show that
{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
preserves compact objects. Using Proposition
4.2.8
, we can reduce to the case
R=P
. Replacing
G
by
P/U_{P}
, we can assume
G=R=P
. Then Lemma
2.1.9
implies
{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
preserves compact objects.

The second claim follows from Proposition
4.2.8
by passing to right adjoints.

∎[Corollary
4.2.13
]

We will also need the right adjoint version of Theorem
4.2.7
. To state it, we will rely on the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2.14
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q_{1}\subseteq Q_{2}\subseteq P\subseteq R_{1}\subseteq R_{2}
, the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{I}}(R_{1},Q_{1})
preserves
P
-tempered objects. In other words, it restricts to a functor

\CT:{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P\supseteq Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P\supseteq Q_{1}).

Proof.

By definition, it suffices to verify that the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})
preserves
P
-tempered objects. This functor factors as
{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})
. The first functor preserves
P
-tempered objects by definition. The second functor preserves
P
-tempered objects because
P
-temperedness for the source and target is defined as
M_{P}
-temperedness, and this functor is
\Sph_{M_{P},x}
-linear.

∎[Lemma
4.2.14
]

Corollary 4.2.15
.

For any morphism from
[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[R_{2}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})
, the functor

\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})

in Theorem
4.2.7
has a continuous right adjoint

\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}:{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})

equivalent to both

{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{1})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{temp}_{P_{1}}}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})

and

{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{temp}_{P_{1}}}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{2})\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}).

In particular, the functor

{\mathcal{W}}(-,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}:\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq P\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)

induces a functor

{\mathcal{W}}(-,-\supseteq-)_{\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}}:\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\DGCat,\;[R\supseteq P\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)

by passing to right adjoints.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.2.7
, the functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})
is equivalent to both

{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{1})\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})

and

{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{1}\supseteq Q_{2})\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}(R_{2},P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}).

Then we are done by Corollary
4.2.13
and Lemma
4.2.14
.

∎[Corollary
4.2.15
]

The following result is proved in §
4.6
:

Proposition 4.2.16
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, let
M_{R}:=R/U_{R}
be the Levi quotient group of
R
. Then the
\Sph_{M_{R},x}
-action on
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}})
preserves the full subcategories

{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{M_{R}}^{Q/U_{R}{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

4.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.8

We can assume
R=G
and
Q\subseteq P
are standard parabolics. Define
P^{-}
and
M:=P\cap P^{-}
as usual.

By definition, we just need to check that the endo-functor
{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
preserves
P
-tempered objects. Using 2nd adjointness on
\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}
(Proposition
4.1.4
), the functor
\CT
is
*
-pull-
!
-push along the diagram

\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

On the other hand, the functor
\Eis
is
*
-pull-
!
-push along the diagramm

\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}

Hence, we have reduced the proposition to the following result:

Lemma 4.3.1
.

Using the above notations, the
*
-pull-
!
-push functor along the diagram

\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}

preserves
M
-tempered objects.

To prove the lemma, we need to study the fiber product

\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,P^{-}\backslash G/P\leftarrow Q\cap M\backslash Q/Q)

and review some geometric objects related to it.

Definition 4.3.2
.

Recall the
relative open Zastava space
defined in
9
9

9

Using the notation of
[
BFGM02
, §3]
, it is the disjoint union of
{}_{0}\!Z_{\Bun_{M}}^{\theta}
for
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
.

[
BFGM02
]
,
[
FFKM99
]
(also see

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}:=\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,P^{-}\backslash G/P\leftarrow{\mathbb{B}}M).

In more familiar words, it classifies
({\mathcal{P}}_{G},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{l},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r},\alpha_{V},\beta_{V})

•

•

•

The composition

.

Its connected components are all smooth. Also recall the absolute open Zastava space
{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P}

{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r}
with the trivial
M
-torsor. We have

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}\simeq\,_{0}\!\Zas_{P}\widetilde{\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}}\Bun_{M}.

We define

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}:=\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,P^{-}\backslash G/P\leftarrow Q\cap M\backslash Q/Q).

In more familiar words, it classifies

;

{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\cap M}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is a generic
(Q\cap M)
-reduction of both

and

, compatible with the maps
\beta_{V}\circ\alpha_{V}
.

More precisely, the maps

are isomorphisms at the generic point of

, hence they provide an identification of the generic

-torsors of

and
{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{l}}

to be compatible with this identification.

We also define the absolute version
{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P}
by replacing
{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r}
in the above definition by the trivial
M
-torsor. We have

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\simeq\,_{0}\!\Zas_{P}\widetilde{\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}}\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Definition 4.3.3
.

Recall the
positive part of the Hecke ind-stack for
M
-torsors
, which we denote
10
10

10

Using the notation of
[

, §3]
, it is the disjoint union of
\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}
for

.
by
\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}
. It classifies
({\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{l},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r},\gamma_{V})
where

•

and

are

-torsors on

;

•

-representation
V
and the corresponding

-representation

\gamma_{V}:W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{r}}}\to W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{l}}}
is an

;

•

The collection of
\{\gamma_{V}\}
should satisfy certain relations which we do not spell out.

Also recall
\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}
is defined similarly by replacing
{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r}
by the trivial
M
-torsor. We have

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\simeq\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\widetilde{\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}}\Bun_{M}.

There is a map

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}\to\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}

sending
({\mathcal{P}}_{G},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{l},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r},\alpha_{V},\beta_{V})
to

.

We can similarly define
\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}
and the map

{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

We also have

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\simeq\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\widetilde{\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}}\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Construction 4.3.4
.

Recall there is a map

(16)

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\to\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}:=\bigsqcup_{\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}X^{\theta},

where

•

\Lambda_{G}^{\mathrm{pos}}\subseteq\Lambda_{G}
is the submonoid spanned by all positive simple coroots in the coweight lattice
\Lambda_{G}
;

•

\Lambda_{G,P}
is the quotient of
\Lambda_{G}
by the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-span of simple coroots contained in
M
;

•

\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
is the image of
\Lambda_{G}^{\mathrm{pos}}
inside
\Lambda_{G,P}
;

•

Each
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
can be uniquely written as the image of
\sum n_{i}\alpha_{i}
for
n_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq 0}
and simple coroots
\alpha_{i}

not
contained in
M
; we write
X^{\theta}:=\prod X^{(n_{i})}
, where
X^{(n_{i})}
is the
n_{i}
-th symmetric product of
X
;

•

Any point of
\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}
can be uniquely written as
\sum\theta_{k}x_{k}
with
\theta_{k}\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
and
x_{k}
being distinct points on
X
, i.e., is a
\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
-colored divisor on
X
.

•

For
({\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{l},{\mathcal{P}}_{M}^{r},\gamma_{V})
as in the definition of
\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}
, the map (
16
) sends it to the unique point
\sum\theta_{k}x_{k}
such that when
W=V^{U_{P}}
is the 1-dimensional representation given by character
\check{\lambda}
, the map
\gamma_{V}:W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{r}}}\to W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{l}}}
induces an isomorphism
W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{r}}}(\sum\langle\theta_{k},\check{\lambda}\rangle\cdot x_{k})\simeq W_{{\mathcal{P}}_{M^{l}}}
.

The similarly defined map

\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\to\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}},

satisfies the following
factorization property
. Let

(\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}})_{\mathrm{disj}}\subseteq\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}

be the open subscheme containing points
(\sum\theta_{k}x_{k},\sum\theta^{\prime}_{l}x^{\prime}_{l})
such that
x_{k},x^{\prime}_{l}
are distinct. Let

(\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}})_{\mathrm{disj}}\subseteq\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}

be the pre-image of this open subscheme. Consider the map

\mathrm{sum}:(\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}})_{\mathrm{disj}}\to\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}

sending
(\sum\theta_{k}x_{k},\sum\theta^{\prime}_{l}x^{\prime}_{l})
to
\sum\theta_{k}x_{k}+\sum\theta^{\prime}_{l}x^{\prime}_{l}
. Then there is a canonical Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}})_{\mathrm{disj}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}})_{\mathrm{disj}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sum}}

where the top arrow is defined using a standard re-gluing construction.

The composition

{}_{0}\Zas_{P}\to\Gr_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}\to\Div_{\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}

also satisfies factorization property.

With these geometric constructions in our toolbox, we are ready to prove Lemma
4.3.1
and therefore finish the proof of Proposition
4.2.8
.

Proof of Lemma
4.3.1
.
Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{}_{0}\!\Zas_{P,\mathrm{rel}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{p_{l}}
\scriptstyle{p_{r}}
\textstyle{\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
\textstyle{\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{l}}
\scriptstyle{h_{r}}
\textstyle{\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}.}

We just need to check that
p_{l,!}\circ p_{r}^{*}
preserves
M
-tempered objects. Using the projection formula, this functor is equivalent to
h_{l,!}({\mathcal{K}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits^{*}}h_{r}^{*}(-))
, where
{\mathcal{K}}
is the
!
-pushforward of the constant sheaf along
\pi
. In other words, it is the functor given by kernel
{\mathcal{K}}
.

Recall we have a map

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\bigsqcup_{\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}X^{\theta}.

Let
{\mathcal{K}}|_{X^{\theta}}
be the restriction of
{\mathcal{K}}
on the pre-image of
X^{\theta}
. Then it remains to prove that the functor given by kernel
{\mathcal{K}}|_{X^{\theta}}
preserves
M
-tempered objects.

Note that
X^{\theta}
has a stratification given by

\bigcup_{\mu\in 0\cup\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}},\mu\leq\theta}(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}x^{\mu}.

This induces a stratification on
\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}|_{X^{\theta}}
. Let
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}x^{\mu}}
be the
*
-restriction of
{\mathcal{K}}|_{X^{\theta}}
to this stratum. Then we just need to prove that the functor given by kernel
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}x^{\mu}}
preserves
M
-tempered objects.

By the factorization properties, we have

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}},\theta,\mu\cdot x}\simeq\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}|_{\mu\cdot x},

and
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}x^{\mu}}
is the
*
-tensor product of
*
-pullbacks of
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}|_{\mu\cdot x}
. We will prove that the functors given by kernels
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}|_{\mu\cdot x}
both preserve
M
-tempered objects.

The claim for the first functor is obvious: Hecke modifications
away
from
x
commute with Hecke modifications at
x
, hence the functor given by kernel
{\mathcal{K}}|_{(X\setminus x)^{\theta-\mu}}
commutes with the
\Sph_{M,x}
-action on
\DMod(\Bun_{M}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}})
.

For the claim for the second functor, note that we have a map

\Hecke_{M,G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{pos}}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}|_{\mu\cdot x}\to\Hecke_{M,x}^{Q\cap M{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Hence the second functor is equivalent to
{\mathcal{L}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M,x}}}-
, where
{\mathcal{L}}
is the
!
-pushforward of
{\mathcal{K}}|_{\mu\cdot x}
along this map. Then we are done because
\Sph_{M,x}^{\mathrm{temp}}
is a two-sided ideal of
\Sph_{M,x}
.

∎[Lemma
4.3.1
]

∎[Proposition
4.2.8
]

4.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.10

We will deduce the proposition from the following result:

Proposition 4.4.1
.

For parabolic subgroups
Q\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)

sends
R
-tempered objects to
P
-tempered objects. In other words, it restricts to a functor

\CT:{\mathcal{W}}(R,R\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q).

Proof.

We can assume
R=G
. The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.0.2
in §
3.5
and we only sketch the main ideas. We just need to construct
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)

such that the composition

{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\adjoint\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)

is equivalent to the adjoint pair
(\Eis,\CT)
. As before, we have a canonical map

\pi:\Gr_{G,x}\widetilde{\times}\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}

and the desired left adjoint is defined by sending
{\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{M}}
to
\pi_{!}({\mathcal{F}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{M}})
.

∎[Proposition
4.4.1
]

Let us finish the proof of Proposition
4.2.10
. Unraveling the definitions, we need to show that if an object in
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
is sent to a
P_{2}
-tempered object by the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P_{2},Q)
, then it is sent to a
P_{1}
-tempered object by the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P_{1},Q)
. Note that the constant term functor
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P_{1},Q)
factors as the following composition of two constant term functors:
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P_{2},Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P_{1},Q)
. Hence the above claim follows from Proposition
4.4.1
(applied to the second functor in the above composition).

∎[Proposition
4.2.10
]

4.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.2.7

Any morphism
[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw^{[2]}(\Par_{G})
factors as

[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[R_{1}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq Q_{2}]\to[R_{1}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]\to[R_{2}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}].

Hence we just need to prove Theorem
4.2.7
in the following three special cases: (1) when
R_{1}=R_{2}=R,P_{1}=P_{2}=P
; (2) when
R_{1}=R_{2}=R,Q_{1}=Q_{2}=Q
; (3) when
P_{1}=P_{2}=P,Q_{1}=Q_{2}=Q
.

For case (1), by definition we need to show that if an object
{\mathcal{F}}
in
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})
is sent to a
P
-tempered object in
{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})
by the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})
, then its image
\Eis({\mathcal{F}})
in
{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2})
is sent to a
P
-tempered object in
{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})
by the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})
. By Lemma
4.5.1
below, it remains to check that the functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})
preserves
P
-tempered objects. But this is obvious because
P
-temperedness for these categories is defined as
M_{P}
-temperedness, and the functor in question is
\Sph_{M_{P},x}
-linear.

Case (2) is just Proposition
4.2.10
.

For case (3), we use the fact that
{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P\supseteq Q)
is generated under colimits by the functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(P,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R_{1},P\supseteq Q)
, see the proof of Corollary
4.2.12
. Hence it suffices to prove that functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R_{2},Q)
preserves
P
-tempered objects. But this is just Proposition
4.2.8
.

∎[Theorem
4.2.7
]

The following “base-change” lemma was used in the above proof and will be used again later:

Lemma 4.5.1
.

For any parabolic subgroups
Q_{1}\subseteq Q_{2}\subseteq P\subseteq R
of
G
, the commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2})}

is right adjointable along the horizontal direction, i.e., we have a canonical commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\CT}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q_{2})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\CT}

Proof.

We can assume
R=G
and the parabolic subgroups are standard. Define
P^{-}
and
M_{P}
as usual. By 2nd adjointness for
\Bun_{G}^{Q_{i}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
(Proposition
4.1.4
), the assertion boils down to proving that the composition of the correspondences

\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q_{1}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

and

\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{2}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

is isomorphic to the composition of the correspondences

\Bun_{G}^{Q_{2}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{G}^{Q_{1}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q_{1}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

and

\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{2}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q_{2}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q_{2}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

The first composition is

\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q_{2}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\leftarrow\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q_{1}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Hence, it remains to prove that the map

\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q_{1}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{Q_{1}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{Q_{2}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{P^{-}}^{Q_{2}\cap M_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

is an isomorphism. This follows from the isomorphism
{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{1}\cap M_{P})\to{\mathbb{B}}Q_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}Q_{2}}}{\mathbb{B}}(Q_{2}\cap M_{P})
.

∎[Lemma
4.5.1
]

4.6.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.16

We can assume
R=G
. In view of Proposition
2.3.15
, the full subcategory
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})
is preserved by the
\Sph_{G,x}
-action. To prove the claim about
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
, we show that, for any
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G,x}
, if the image of
{\mathcal{M}}
under the functor
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
is
M_{P}
-tempered, then so is the image of
{\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G,x}}}{\mathcal{M}}
. Recall in the proof of Proposition
4.4.1
, we have factorized this functor
\CT
as

{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{u}\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\xrightarrow{v}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q).

By construction, the left adjoint of the first functor
u
is
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear, hence so is
u
(see Appendix
A
). Thus, it remains to show that, for any
{\mathcal{N}}\in\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Sph_{G,x}
, if
v({\mathcal{N}})
is
M_{P}
-tempered, then so is
v({\mathcal{F}}\mathbin{\mathop{\star}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{G,x}}}{\mathcal{N}})
.

The proof below works for any
\Sph_{M_{P},x}
-module category
{\mathcal{C}}
and in particular for
{\mathcal{C}}:={\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
. Consider the obvious commutative diagram

\textstyle{\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{M_{P}\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}
\scriptstyle{\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\oblv_{M_{P}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}^{M_{P}\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oblv_{M_{P}}}

This square is left adjointable along the horizontal direction because
\iota_{P,x}^{!}
has a
\Sph_{M_{P},x}
-linear left adjoint
(\iota_{P,x})_{!}
. It follows that this square is also right adjointble along the vertical direction. In other words, we have a commutative square
