\textstyle{\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}
\scriptstyle{\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\mathrm{temp}_{M_{P}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{temp}_{M_{P}}}
\textstyle{\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{M_{P}\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}^{M_{P}\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}.}

Now, for any object
{\mathcal{N}}\in\SI_{P,x}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{\Sph_{M_{P},x}}}{\mathcal{C}}
, the object
(\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id)({\mathcal{N}})
is
M_{P}
-tempered iff the monad
\oblv_{M_{P}}\circ\mathrm{temp}_{M_{P}}
acts on it trivially, iff the morphism

(\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\oblv_{M_{P}}\circ\mathrm{temp}_{M_{P}})({\mathcal{N}})\to{\mathcal{N}}

is sent to an isomorphism by
\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id
. Since the last functor is conservative (because its left adjoint generate the target under colimits), we see
(\iota_{P,x}^{!}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id)({\mathcal{N}})
is
M_{P}
-tempered iff
(\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\oblv_{M_{P}}\circ\mathrm{temp}_{M_{P}})({\mathcal{N}})\to{\mathcal{N}}
is an isomorphism. The latter condition is clearly preserved by the
\Sph_{G,x}
-action on
{\mathcal{N}}
, hence we are done.

∎[Proposition
4.2.16
]

5.
The automorphic gluing theorem

In this section, we state our main theorem and perform several reduction steps.

In §
5.1
, we give the precise formulation of the main theorem (Theorem
5.1.2
), which states that
\DMod(\Bun_{G})
can be glued from
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
, where
[P\supseteq Q]
ranges over
twisted arrows
in the category of standard parabolic subgroups of
G
.

We also give an anti-tempered version of the main theorem (Theorem
5.1.4
), which says that the category
\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
can be glued from
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
, where
R
ranges over
proper
standard parabolic subgroups of
G
.

We will reduce the main theorem to its anti-tempered version, and prove the latter in §
6
.

In §
5.2
, to achieve this reduction, we state the automorphic gluing for the parabolic category, which says that
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)
can be glued from
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
, where
[P\supseteq Q]
ranges over twisted arrows in the category of standard parabolic subgroups of
G
contained in
R
. We prove (see Proposition
5.2.2
) that this result follows from our main theorem applied to the Levi quotient group of
R
, which is a reductive group whose rank is smaller than
G
.

In §
5.3
, using induction and the above result, we deduce our main theorem from its anti-tempered version.

5.1.
Statement of the main theorem

Construction 5.1.1
.

Let
\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})
be the category of twisted arrows between
standard
parabolic subgroups of
G
, i.e., the objects of
\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})
are standard parabolic subgroups
[P\supseteq Q]
, and there is a unique morphism from
[P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
iff
Q_{1}\subseteq Q_{2}\subseteq P_{2}\subseteq P_{1}
.

By Theorem
4.2.7
and Proposition
4.2.16
, we have a functor

{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}:\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})\to\Sph_{G,x}\hmod,\;[P\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q),

where a morphism from
[P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw(\Par_{G})
is sent to the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\to{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}).

View
{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}
as a diagram in
\Sph_{G,x}\hmod
and consider its colimit. We have a functor

\gamma_{G}^{L}:\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)=\DMod(\Bun_{G})

whose restriction to each
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
is the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{I}}(G,G).

By Corollary
4.2.15
and Appendix
A
, we can pass to right adjoints and obtain a functor

{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}}:\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\Sph_{G,x}\hmod,\;[P\supseteq Q]\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q),

where a morphism from
[P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]
to
[P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw(\Par_{G})
is sent to the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}:{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\to{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}).

We have a functor

\gamma_{G}:\DMod(\Bun_{G})={\mathcal{I}}(G,G)\to\lim{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}}

whose value at each
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
is the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{temp}_{P}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q).

Define

\Glue_{G}:=\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}\simeq\lim{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}}.

Then we obtain
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\gamma_{G}^{L}:\Glue_{G}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G}):\gamma_{G}

The functor
\gamma_{G}
(resp.
\gamma_{G}^{L}
) is called the
automorphic gluing functor
(resp.
automorphic de-gluing functor
).

The following result is the main theorem of this paper:

Theorem 5.1.2

(Automorphic gluing)
.

The adjoint functors

\gamma_{G}^{L}:\Glue_{G}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G}):\gamma_{G}

are inverse to each other.

In §
5.3
, we will reduce the theorem to its
G
-anti-tempered version, which is stated below.

Construction 5.1.3
.

Define

\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:=\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),R\neq G}\,}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\simeq\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}},

where the connecting functors in the colimit (resp. limit) are the functors
\Eis^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
(resp.
\CT^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
).

We have a functor

\beta_{G}^{L}:\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),R\neq G}\,}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

whose restriction to each
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
is the functor

\Eis^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}.

We similarly define the functor

\beta_{G}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\to\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

and obtain adjoint functors

\beta_{R}^{L}:\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:\beta_{R}.

The following theorem is proved in
\lx@sectionsign

6
.

Theorem 5.1.4

(Automorphic gluing for anti-tempered objects)
.

The adjoint functors

\beta_{R}^{L}:\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:\beta_{R}

are inverse to each other.

Warning 5.1.5
.

In Construction
5.1.3
, we require
R\neq G
. Otherwise the theorem is trivial.

Warning 5.1.6
.

The functor

\DMod(\Bun_{G})\to\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)

is
not
an equivalence. For example, when
G=T
is a torus, the RHS is the zero category.

Warning 5.1.7
.

Recall
\Glue_{G}
is also equipped with a
\Sph_{G,x}
-action, hence we can define its full subcategory
\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
of
G
-anti-tempered objects, which
a priori
is different from
\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
. However, once Theorem
5.1.2
and Theorem
5.1.4
are proven, it will be clear that these two categories are equivalent.

5.2.
Automorphic gluing for the parabolic categories
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)

In this subsection, we fix
R\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
with
R\neq G
and let
M_{R}:=R/U_{R}
be its Levi quotient group.

To deduce Theorem
5.1.2
from Theorem
5.1.4
, we need to de-glue
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
into pieces. In fact, such de-gluing exists even for
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)
. In this subsection, we describe and prove such de-gluing under the assumption that Theorem
5.1.2
is correct for all smaller rank reductive groups.

Construction 5.2.1
.

Similarly to Construction
5.1.1
, let
\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})\subseteq\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})
be the full category of twisted arrows between standard parabolic subgroups of
G
contained in
R
. Define

\Glue_{R}:=\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\Eis}|_{\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\simeq\lim{\mathcal{W}}(G,-\supseteq-)_{\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}}|_{\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}.

In other words,

\Glue_{R}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\simeq\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q),

where the connecting functors in the colimit (resp. limit) are the functors
\Eis
(resp.
\CT_{\mathrm{temp}}
).

We have a functor

\gamma_{R}^{L}:\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)

whose restriction to each
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
is the
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear functor

{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R).

We similarly define the functor

\gamma_{R}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\to\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)

and obtain
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear adjoint functors

\gamma_{R}^{L}:\Glue_{R}\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,R):\gamma_{R}.

Proposition 5.2.2

(Automorphic Gluing for
{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)
)
.

Suppose the adjoint functors

\gamma_{M_{R}}^{L}:\Glue_{M_{R}}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{M_{R}}):\gamma_{M_{R}}

are inverse to each other (i.e. Theorem
5.1.2
is known for
M_{R}
), then the adjoint functors

\gamma_{R}^{L}:\Glue_{R}\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,R):\gamma_{R}

are inverse to each other.

Proof.

According to our notation,
{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)=\DMod(\Bun_{M_{R}})
and we have adjoint functors

\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\adjoint{\mathcal{I}}(G,R):\CT.

By Lemma
2.3.7
, this adjoint pair is monadic.

On the other hand, by Theorem
4.2.7
, we have a functor

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)

induced by
\Eis:{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
. By definition, this gives a functor
\Glue_{M_{R}}\to\Glue_{R}
, which we still denote by

\Eis:\Glue_{M_{R}}\to\Glue_{R}.

Passing to right adjoints, by Corollary
4.2.15
, we obtain a functor

\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\to\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)

induced by
\CT:{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)
. By definition, this gives a functor
\Glue_{R}\to\Glue_{M_{R}}
, which we still denote by

\CT:\Glue_{R}\to\Glue_{M_{R}}.

By construction, these two functors are adjoint to each other:

\Eis:\Glue_{M_{R}}\adjoint\Glue_{R}:\CT.

We claim the right adjoint is conservative. To prove the claim, we only need to show each
\CT:{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\to{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)
is conservative. By definition, we only need to show
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)
is conservative. But this follows from the fact that both
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(Q,Q)
and
\CT:{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(Q,Q)
are conservative (Lemma
2.3.7
). This proves the claim that
\CT:\Glue_{M_{R}}\to\Glue_{R}
is conservative. In other words, we have shown that

\CT:\Glue_{R}\to\Glue_{M_{R}}

is also monadic.

Combining the above two paragraphs, we only need to show the monads

{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\to{\mathcal{I}}(R,R),\;\Glue_{M_{R}}\to\Glue_{R}\to\Glue_{M_{R}}

are compatible with the equivalence
\gamma_{M_{R}}:{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\simeq\Glue_{M_{R}}
. More precisely, by Corollary
4.2.15
, we have a commuative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(R,P\supseteq Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{M_{R}}^{L}}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{R}^{L}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,R),}

and we only need to show it is right adjointable along the vertical direction. By definition, we only need to show that, for any
[P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1}]\to[P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2}]
in
\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})
, the commutative squares

\textstyle{{\mathcal{W}}(R,P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{W}}(R,P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{1}\supseteq Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{W}}(G,P_{2}\supseteq Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)}

are right adjointable along the vertical direction. By Lemma
4.2.14
, we only need to prove similar thing for

\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(R,R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,R).}

Then we are done by Lemma
4.5.1
.

∎[Proposition
5.2.2
]

5.3.
Deducing the main theorem from its anti-tempered version

In this subsection, we deduce Theorem
5.1.2
from Theorem
5.1.4
.

To prove Theorem
5.1.2
, we proceed by induction on the (semi-simple) rank of the reductive group
G
. When
\Rank(G)=0
, i.e.
G=T
is a torus, we only need to show
\DMod(\Bun_{T})\simeq{\mathcal{W}}(T,T\supseteq T)
. But this is obvious because any object is
T
-tempered.

Now we assume
\Rank(G)>0
and that Theorem
5.1.2
is correct for any reductive group with smaller rank. In particular, the assumption of Proposition
5.2.2
is satisfied.

To continue, we need the following useful lemma:

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
be
\Sph_{G}
-module categories and

L:{\mathcal{C}}\adjoint{\mathcal{D}}:R

be
\Sph_{G}
-linear adjoint functors. Then
L
and
R
are equivalences iff the induced functors

L^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\adjoint{\mathcal{D}}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:R^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}},\;L^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\adjoint{\mathcal{D}}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:R^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

are equivalences.

Proof.

The “only if” part is obvious. For the “if” part, we just need to prove that the adjunction natural transformations
L\circ R\to\Id_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\Id_{\mathcal{D}}\to R\circ L
are invertible. Since any object
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
(resp.
y\in{\mathcal{D}}
) is an extension of its anti-temperization by its temperization, we only need to show
L\circ R(x)\to x
(resp.
y\to R\circ L(y)
) is invertible when
x
(resp.
y
) is either tempered or anti-tempered. If
x
is tempered, we have
L\circ R(x)\simeq L^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\circ R^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}(x)
, and the RHS is equivalent to
x
by assumption. The other cases are similar.

∎[Lemma
5.3.1
]

By the lemma, we just need to show that both

(\gamma_{G}^{L})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:(\gamma_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}

and

(\gamma_{G}^{L})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:(\gamma_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

are mutually inverse functors. The
G
-tempered case is almost obvious:

Lemma 5.3.2
.

The adjoint functors

(\gamma_{G}^{L})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}:(\gamma_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}

are inverse to each other.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.2.10
, for any
[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})
, we have
{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
. By definition
{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)={\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}
. It follows that
{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)\simeq{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}
as full subcategories of
{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)
.

Hence we have

\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{temp}}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q).

Note that the terms in the RHS do not depend on
P
. In other words, we have a colimit diagram that is constant along the fibers of the forgetful functor
\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})\to\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},\;[P\supseteq Q]\mapsto Q
. Since these fibers are weakly contractible (because they have final objects), we have

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}}\,}{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq Q)\simeq{\mathcal{W}}(G,G\supseteq G)=\DMod(\Bun_{G})

as desired.

∎[Lemma
5.3.2
]

For the
G
-anti-tempered case, by Theorem
5.1.4
and Proposition
5.2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}\Glue_{R}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\neq G}\big(\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{R}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\supseteq P,R\neq G}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\big).

Note that the inner limit diagram in the RHS is a constant one (i.e. does not depend on
R
), and the index category
\big\{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}\,\big|\,R\supseteq P,R\neq G\big\}\subseteq(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}
is weakly contractible because
P
is a final object. Hence we have

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(\lim_{R\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},R\supseteq P,R\neq G}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{[P\supseteq Q]\in\Tw(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathcal{W}}(G,P\supseteq Q)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}.

It follows from the construction that the underlying functor of this equivalence is
\gamma_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
. Hence

(\gamma_{G}^{L})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:\Glue_{G}^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\adjoint\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}:(\gamma_{G})^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

are mutually inverse equivalences as desired.

∎[Theorem
5.1.2
]

6.
Proof of the anti-tempered automorphic gluing theorem

In this section, we prove Theorem
5.1.4
. We need to show that
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}\to\Id
and
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
are both equivalences.

In §
6.2
, we prove the equivalence
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}\simeq\Id
using the
Deligne–Lusztig duality on
\Bun_{G}
, which is reviewed in §
6.1
.

The rest of this section (from §
6.3
to §
6.8
) is devoted to proving the equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
.

In §
6.3
, we reduce it to the calculation of a certain colimit. More precisely, for any family
({\mathcal{M}}_{P}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}})
compatible with the
\CT
functors, we need to show that

\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\in\DMod(\Bun_{L}).

Here
R
is a maximal proper parabolic subgroup of
G
, with Levi quotient group
L
, and
\iota_{L}^{!}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\to\DMod(\Bun_{M})
is the functor defined in §
2.3
.

Let us first describe our strategy for this calculation:

Strategy 6.0.1
.

The strategy consists of three major steps.

For each proper parabolic
P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
, write
\alpha_{P}:=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
. In the first step, we define a compatible
11
11

11

that is, functorial in
P
exhaustive (increasing) filtration
\alpha_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}
on each
\alpha_{P}
. The filtration is labelled by elements
\overline{w}
in the poset
W_{G}/W_{R}
, where
W_{G}
(resp.
W_{R}
) is the Weyl group of
G
(resp.
L
) and the partial order on
W_{G}/W_{R}
is the standard one induced from the Bruhat order.

In the second step, we prove the
cancellation lemma
: for any triple
(Q\subseteq P,\overline{w})
satisfying certain purely combinatorial conditions, the morphism
\alpha_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{Q})\to\alpha_{P}^{=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})
is invertible. Here,
\alpha_{P}^{=\overline{w}}
denotes the
\overline{w}
-graded piece of the filtration on
\alpha_{P}
.

Finally, in the third step, we use the cancellation lemma and some more Weyl combinatorics to show that

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq\begin{cases}0&\mbox{for $\overline{w}\neq\overline{1}$};\\
\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})&\mbox{for $\overline{w}=\overline{1}$}.\end{cases}

Remark 6.0.2
.

Thus, the starting point of the above strategy is the filtration on
\alpha_{P}:=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
. However, its construction is subtle: we will actually construct two filtrations, the
Weyl filtration
and the
dual Weyl filtration
. The former is more natural, but for technical reasons (see below) we are not able to prove the relative cancellation lemma. On the other hand, the dual Weyl filtration is more complicated to define (it requires miraculous parabolic duality), but it allows us to successfully apply the above strategy. It is quite possible that these two filtrations coincide.

Let us continue with the summary of the contents of the present section. In §
6.4
, we define the
Weyl filtration
on each
\alpha_{P}
. This filtration is induced by a filtration on the prestack

\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R}.

We will then provide a
non-rigorous proof
of the corresponding cancellation lemma. It is non-rigorous because we assume that certain averaging functors (which are right adjoints of the standard functors, i.e., the forgetful functors) commute with
!
-pushforward functors
(which are left adjoints of the standard functors, i.e., the
!
-pullback functors). In general, it is hard to prove commutativity between left adjoints and right adjoints. For this reason, we
cannot
make this proof rigorous, but we decided to include it anyway for pedagogical reasons and because we believe it can made it work once more technology is established.

To overcome the above issues, we use categorical duality. Namely, in §
6.6
, we consider the
dual
Weyl filtration
\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}
on
\alpha_{P}:=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
induced by the Weyl filtration on its
dual functor

\alpha_{P}^{\vee}\simeq\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}
. Here this identification is due to the
parabolic miraculous duality

{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{\vee}\simeq{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
of
[
Che21
]
, which we review in §
6.5
; the Weyl filtration on
\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}
is again induced by a filtration of geometric objects.

In §
6.7
, we state and prove the cancellation lemma for the dual Weyl filtrations. This can be rigorously proved because we only need to use
!
-pullback and
*
-pushforward functors.

In §
6.8
, we calculate each graded piece:

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq\begin{cases}\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})&\mbox{for $\overline{w}=\overline{1}$};\\
0&\mbox{for $\overline{w}=\overline{w}_{\circ}$};\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\\
\end{cases}

As mentioned in Strategy
6.0.1
, this colimit will be calculated (in the first and third case) using the cancellation lemma and Weyl combinatorics. The case when
\overline{w}=\overline{w}_{\circ}
is the longest element deserves special attention and quite different methods: we will deduce its vanishing from Deligne–Lusztig duality and Theorem
3.0.2
.

6.1.
Recollection: the Deligne–Lusztig duality

In this subsection, we recall the Deligne–Lusztig duality on
\Bun_{G}
proved in
[
Che20a
]
. This is one of the main ingredients in the proof of the equivalence
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}\simeq\Id
.

Construction 6.1.1
.

Consider the objects

\Delta_{!}(k),\Delta_{*}(\omega)\in\DMod(\Bun_{G}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Bun_{G}),

where
\Delta:\Bun_{G}\to\Bun_{G}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Bun_{G}
is the diagonal map and
k
(resp.
\omega
) is the constant (resp. dualizing) D-module. Via the equivalence

\DMod(\Bun_{G}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Bun_{G})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{G})\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits_{k}}\DMod(\Bun_{G})\simeq\LFun_{k}(\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{\vee},\DMod(\Bun_{G})),

we obtain functors between DG categories:

\PsId_{G,!},\PsId_{G,*}:\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{\vee}\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}).

The following result is the main theorem of
[
Gai17
]
:

Theorem 6.1.2

(Miraculous duality on
\Bun_{G}
)
.

The functor
\PsId_{G,!}:\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{\vee}\to\DMod(\Bun_{G})
is an equivalence.

As a consequence, we obtain an endo-functor

\PsId_{G,*}\circ\PsId_{G,!}^{-1}

on the category
\DMod(\Bun_{G})={\mathcal{I}}(G,G)
.

On the other hand, we have the following construction:

Construction 6.1.3
.

The functor (see Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
)

{\mathcal{I}}(G,-)_{\Eis}:\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\to\DGCat,\;R\mapsto{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)

induces an endo-functor

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{R\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},R\neq G}\,}\Eis_{R\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}

on
{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)
equipped with a natural transformation to the identity functor
\Id
.

The main theorem of
[
Che20a
]
states:

Theorem 6.1.4

(Deligne–Lusztig duality on
\Bun_{G}
)
.

Up to a cohomological shift, the functor
\PsId_{G,*}\circ\PsId_{G,!}^{-1}
is equivalent to

\mathrm{DL}_{G}:=\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{R\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},R\neq G}\,}\Eis_{R\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to\Id).

6.2.
The equivalence
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}\simeq\Id

The goal of this section is to prove
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}\simeq\Id
.

We need to show that, for
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
, the cofiber of
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
vanishes. We prove this by showing that such cofiber is both
G
-tempered and
G
-anti-tempered.

By definition, we have

\beta_{G}^{L}\circ\beta_{G}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{R\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},R\neq G}\,}\Eis_{R\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{F}}).

Hence by the Deligne–Lusztig duality (Theorem
6.1.4
), the desired cofiber is isomorphic to
\PsId_{G,*}\circ\PsId_{G,!}^{-1}({\mathcal{F}})
up to a shift.

By
[
DG15
, §4.4.3]
, the image of the functor
\PsId_{G,*}
is contained in
\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
. By
[
Ber21b
, Theorem B]
, we have
\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}}\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G})^{G\mathrm{-temp}}
. Hence the object
\PsId_{G,*}\circ\PsId_{G,!}^{-1}({\mathcal{F}})
is
G
-tempered as desired.

On the other hand, since
\Eis_{R\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
and
\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{F}})
are
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear, the objects
{\mathcal{F}}
and
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}({\mathcal{F}})
are both
G
-anti-tempered. Hence their cofiber is also
G
-anti-tempered as desired.

Combining the above two paragraphs, we obtain that
\beta^{L}_{G}\circ\beta_{G}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism for any
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,G)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
.

6.3.
The equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
: reduction to a colimit calculation

The goal of the rest of this section is to prove
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
. In this subsection, we reduce it to a colimit calculation (see Goal
6.3.1
below).

By definition, an object of

\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\simeq\lim_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},P\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

is a family of objects
{\mathcal{M}}_{P}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
for
P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},P\neq G,
compatible with the CT functors. From now on, we fix such a family and denote the corresponding object by
{\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet}\in\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}
. Our goal is to show that the arrow
{\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet}\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}({\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet})
is invertible. For this, we need to prove that
{\mathcal{M}}_{P}\to\ev_{P}\circ\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}({\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet})
is invertible for any
P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},P\neq G
, where

\ev_{P}:\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\simeq\lim_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}},P\neq G}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}

is the evaluation functor. Clearly, it suffices to check that

{\mathcal{M}}_{R}\to\ev_{R}\circ\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}({\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet})

is invertible for any
maximal (proper)
standard parabolic subgroup
R
of
G
. From now on, we fix such an
R
and denote its Levi quotient group by
L
.

Note that
\ev_{P}
has a left adjoint (the inserting functor)

\ins_{P}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}\simeq\Glue_{G\mathrm{-}\mathrm{atemp}}.

It follows from the definitions that

{\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet}\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\ins_{P}\circ\ev_{P}({\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\ins_{P}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

Thus, we obtain

\ev_{R}\circ\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}({\mathcal{M}}_{\bullet})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\ev_{R}\circ\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}\circ\ins_{P}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}),

where for
P_{1}\subseteq P_{2}
, the connecting morphism is given by

\displaystyle\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P_{1}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P_{1}})\simeq\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P_{1}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{P_{2}\leftarrow P_{1}}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P_{2}})\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P_{2}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P_{1}\to P_{2}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{P_{2}\leftarrow P_{1}}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P_{2}})\to\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P_{2}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P_{2}}).

Hence, we only need to show that the composition

{\mathcal{M}}_{R}\to\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})

is invertible. Since the functor
\iota_{L}^{!}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\to\DMod(\Bun_{L})
is conservative, it suffices to check that the arrow

\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\iota_{L}^{!}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})

is invertible. By Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
,
\iota_{L}^{!}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\CT_{R,*}
. Hence, we have reduced our proof to the following goal:

Goal 6.3.1
.

We need to show the morphism

\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in(\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}),P\neq G}\,}\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})

is invertible.

6.4.
The equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
: the Weyl filtration

In this subsection, we introduce the Weyl filtration on the functor

\alpha_{P}:=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}

and provide a non-rigorous proof for the cancellation lemma.

By definition,
\alpha_{P}
is the restriction of

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L})

to the full subcategory
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})
. By Remark
2.3.1
and the pseudo-proper base-change theorem (see
[
Gai15a
, Corollary 1.5.4]
), the above composition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\delta_{P}:\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{R})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L}).

Clearly, we have:

\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R}\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,{\mathbb{B}}R\leftarrow P\backslash G/R).

We are going to define a filtration on this prestack. In plain words, this prestack classifies
G
-torsors on
X
equipped with an
R
-structure and a generic
P
-structure; then, roughly speaking, the filtration constructed below measures the relative position of the generic
R
-torsor and
P
-torsor inside the generic
G
-torsor.

Construction 6.4.1
.

The double quotient
P\backslash G/R
has a stratification labelled by
W_{P}\backslash W_{G}/W_{R}
, where
W_{P}
(resp.
W_{R}
,
W_{G}
) is the Weyl group for the its Levi subgroup. Namely, for any
w\in W_{G}
, we have a stratum
12
12

12

To simplify the notations, we fix a section
W_{G}=N_{G}(T)/T\to N_{G}(T)
.

P\backslash PwR/R
which only depends on the image of
w
in
W_{P}\backslash W_{G}/W_{R}
.

Consider the standard partial order on
W_{G}/W_{R}
induced by the Bruhat order of
W_{G}
. In other words, for
\overline{w}_{1},\overline{w}_{2}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, we have the following equivalent conditions:

•

\overline{w}_{1}\leq\overline{w}_{2}
;

•

For any representatives
w_{1},w_{2}\in W_{G}
of
\overline{w}_{1},\overline{w}_{2}
, the orbit
Bw_{1}R
is contained in the closure of
Bw_{2}R
;

•

For representatives
w_{1},w_{2}\in W_{G}
of
\overline{w}_{1},\overline{w}_{2}
that are maximal for the Bruhat order,
w_{1}\leq w_{2}
.

For any
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and any representative
w\in W_{G}
of it, consider the closure
\overline{PwR}
of
PwR
in
G
. Note that it only depends on
\overline{w}
. Define

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}}:=\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,{\mathbb{B}}R\leftarrow P\backslash\overline{PwR}/R)

Then for
\overline{w}_{1}\leq\overline{w}_{2}
, we have a map

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}_{1}}\to(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}_{2}}.

This is a closed embedding (up to non-reduced structures) because
\overline{Pw_{1}R}\to\overline{Pw_{2}R}
is a closed embedding. Note that, for the longest element
w_{\circ}
in
W_{G}
, we have

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}_{\circ}}=(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R}).

Hence, we obtain a filtration of
\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R}
indexed by the poset
W_{G}/W_{R}
.

Construction 6.4.2
.

The above filtration on
\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R}
induces a filtration on the functor
\delta_{P}
such that
\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}
is the composition

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{R})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L}).

Note that we have
\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}_{\circ}}=\delta_{P}
. We obtain a filtration on
\alpha_{P}
by restricting to
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})
.

Construction 6.4.3
.

The above filtrations are compatible with the connecting morphisms in
\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P}\,}\alpha_{P}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})
, i.e., compatible with the natural transfomations

(17)

\displaystyle\alpha_{Q}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{Q\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to

\displaystyle\to\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}=\alpha_{P}.

In other words, we have canonical natural transformations

\alpha_{Q}^{\leq\overline{w}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to\alpha_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}.

In more detail, for
Q\subseteq P
, consider the map
p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}}:\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. Recall (see Definition
2.3.16
) that

\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}.

Hence we have a natural transformation

(18)

\oblv^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}

between functors
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})
.

On the other hand, the obvious commutative sqaure

\textstyle{(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{\leq\overline{w}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}}

induces a natural transformation

(19)

\delta_{Q}^{\leq\overline{w}}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}\to\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}.

Combining with the previous paragraph, we obtain the desired natural transformation

(20)

\alpha_{Q}^{\leq\overline{w}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\delta_{Q}^{\leq\overline{w}}\circ\oblv^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to\delta_{Q}^{\leq\overline{w}}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}\to\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}\simeq\alpha_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}.

Construction 6.4.4
.

For any
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, we define

\delta_{P}^{<\overline{w}}:=\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{\overline{w}^{\prime}<\overline{w}}\,}\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}^{\prime}},\;\delta_{P}^{=\overline{w}}:=\mathrm{coFib}(\delta_{P}^{<\overline{w}}\to\delta_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}).

It follows from the definitions that:

•

If
\overline{w}
is minimal in the fiber of
W_{G}/W_{R}\to W_{P}\backslash W_{G}/W_{R}
containing it, then

\displaystyle\delta_{P}^{=\overline{w}}:\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{R})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L});

moreover

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,{\mathbb{B}}R\leftarrow P\backslash PwR/R).

•

If
\overline{w}
is not minimal in the fiber of
W_{G}/W_{R}\to W_{P}\backslash W_{G}/W_{R}
containing it, then
\delta_{P}^{=\overline{w}}\simeq 0
.

Restricting to
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
, we obtain the functors
\alpha_{P}^{=\overline{w}}
.

Remark 6.4.5
.

Write
R^{w}:=wRw^{-1}
. Note that
P\backslash PwR/R\simeq{\mathbb{B}}(P\cap R^{w})
and
{\mathbb{B}}R\simeq{\mathbb{B}}R^{w}
. Hence we have

(21)

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}\simeq\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(P\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}.

Since any inner automorphism of
G
induces the identity map on
{\mathbb{B}}G
, the isomorphism
{\mathbb{B}}R\simeq{\mathbb{B}}R^{w}
is defined over
{\mathbb{B}}G
. It follows that via (
21
), the map
(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}\to\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
is given by the natural map
\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(P\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}
induced by
R^{w}\to G
.

Definition 6.4.6
.

For fixed
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, let
\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}\subseteq\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\big\{G\big\}
be the sub-poset of parabolics
P
such that
\overline{w}
is minimal in the fiber of
W_{G}/W_{R}\to W_{P}\backslash W_{G}/W_{R}
containing it.

Now we state the cancellation lemma (or in fact conjecture) for the Weyl filtration on the functor
\alpha_{P}:=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
. We write it as a conjecture and provide a non-rigorous proof as a remark.

Conjecture 6.4.7

(Cancellation lemma for the Weyl filtration)
.

For fixed
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and any representative
w
of it, let
Q,P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
with
Q\subseteq P
satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

Q\cap R^{w}=P\cap R^{w}
;

(ii)

U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
, where
U_{Q}
(resp.
U_{R}
) is the unipotent radical of
Q
(resp.
R
),
M_{P}
is the Levi subgroup of
P
and
U_{R}^{w}:=wU_{R}w^{-1}
.

Then (
20
) induces an equivalence

\alpha_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\alpha_{P}^{=\overline{w}}

between functors
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{L})

Remark 6.4.8

(A non-rigorous proof)
.

By Condition (i) and Remark
6.4.5

(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}\simeq(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}.

We claim this implies the natural transformation

\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}\to\delta_{P}^{=\overline{w}}

induced by (
19
) is invertible. Indeed, unwinding the definitions, we only need to show that the following commutative square (given by the usual base-change isomorphism)

\textstyle{\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{*\mathrm{-push}}
\textstyle{\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!\mathrm{-pull}}
\scriptstyle{*\mathrm{-push}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!\mathrm{-pull}}

is left adjointable along horizontal direction. But this follows from the construction of pseudo-proper
!
-pushforward (see
[
Gai15a
, Corollary 1.5.4]
).

Hence it remains to show that the natural transformation

\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ\oblv^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ\oblv^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}\to\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}|_{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}

induced by (
18
) is invertible. Now
imagine
the adelic group
U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})
exists in algebraic geometry and the analogy in Remark
2.3.6
can be made precise. For any object
{\mathcal{M}}_{P}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
, since it is
U_{P}({\mathbb{A}})
-equivariant, so is
(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})
. Note that
U_{Q}/U_{P}\simeq U_{Q}\cap M_{P}
. Hence

\oblv^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq\oblv^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

Hence we only need to show

\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\to\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\circ\oblv_{*}^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}

is invertible. Note that the map

\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(Q\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}\simeq(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{R})^{=\overline{w}}\to\Bun_{L}

factors through
\Bun_{L^{w}}^{(Q\cap L^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}
. We can replace
\delta_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}
by the composition

\delta^{\prime}:\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(Q\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}}^{(Q\cap L^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}).

By Condition (ii),
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq\ker(Q\cap R^{w}\to Q\cap L^{w})
, hence
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})
also acts on
\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(Q\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}
and stabilizes the fibers of
\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(Q\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{L^{w}}^{(Q\cap L^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}
. This indicates

\delta^{\prime}\simeq\delta^{\prime}\circ\oblv_{*}^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}

.

The above argument is non-rigorous at least for the following two reasons:

(1)

We did not define the pushforward functor along
\Bun_{R^{w}}^{(Q\cap R^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{L^{w}}^{(Q\cap L^{w}){\mathrm{-gen}}}
, which is neither pseudo-proper nor represented by algebraic stacks;

(2)

We did not define the adelic-actions and verify various properties of the averaging functors.

Problem (1) is not essential. But Problem (2) seems out of reach with our current techniques.

6.5.
Recollection: the parabolic miraculous duality
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{\vee}\simeq{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

As explained in the beginning of this section, we need to use the duality between
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
. The following result is a global analogue of Theorem
3.1.2
and a generalization of the miraculous duality on
\Bun_{G}
(Theorem
6.1.2
):

Theorem 6.5.1

(​​
[
Che21
, Theorem E, Theorem 5.3.5]
)
.

Let
P
and
P^{-}
be opposite parabolic subgroups of
G
and
M:=P\cap P^{-}
be their Levi subgroup. Then the categories
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
are canonically dual to each other. Moreover,

(1)

In the case
P=P^{-}=G
, we recover the miraculous duality on
\Bun_{G}
;

(2)

Via this duality and the miraculous duality on
\Bun_{G}
, the functors

\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}),\;\;\Eis_{P^{-}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})\to\DMod(\Bun_{G})

are conjugate to each other;

(3)

Via this duality and the miraculous duality on
\Bun_{M}
, the functors

\iota_{M,!}:\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P),\;\;\iota_{M,!}^{-}:\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

are conjugate to each other.

As in the local case (see §
3.1
), the above theorem formally implies:

Corollary 6.5.2
.

Via the above dualities, we have:

(1)

The functors

\displaystyle\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{G}),\;\;\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}:\DMod(\Bun_{G})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

are dual to each other;

(2)

The functors

\displaystyle\iota_{M}^{!}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{M}),\;\;\iota_{M,!}^{-}:\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

are dual to each other;

(3)

The functors

\displaystyle\iota_{M}^{*}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{M}),\;\;\iota_{M,*}^{-}:\DMod(\Bun_{M})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

are dual to each other.

Remark 6.5.3
.

In fact, for any
13
13

13

Except in this remark, we always use
Q
to denote a parabolic subgroup
smaller
than
P
. We give up this convention here to be compatible with
[
Che21
]
.

P\subseteq Q
and
P^{-}\subseteq Q^{-}
, the functors

\displaystyle\Eis_{P\to Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q),\;\;\CT_{Q^{-}\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q^{-})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})

are also dual to each other via the above dualities. This is implicit in
[
Che21
]
. Let us explain the main ideas but treat the constructions in
loc.cit.
as a blackbox.

We can assume
P
and
Q
are standard. In
[
Che21
, §5.3]
(see also
[
Che20a
]
), the author considered a certain algebraic stack
\overline{\Bun}_{G}
equipped with a closed filtration
(\overline{\Bun}_{G})_{\leq P}
labelled by elements
P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
. There is a certain functor

{\mathbf{K}}:\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\to\DMod(\overline{\Bun}_{G})

such that, for each
P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
, the object
{\mathbf{K}}(P)
is supported on the closed substack
(\overline{\Bun}_{G})_{\leq P}
of
\overline{\Bun}_{G}
. There are maps

\overline{\Delta}^{\mathrm{enh}}_{\leq P}:(\overline{\Bun}_{G})_{\leq P}\to\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

functorial in
P
. The
!
-pushforward of
{\mathbf{K}}(P)
along this map, which we denote by
{\mathcal{K}}_{P}
, is contained in the full subcategory

{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})\simeq{\mathcal{I}}(G\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}G,P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}P^{-})\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

Then
[
Che21
, Theorem 5.3.5]
says that
{\mathcal{K}}_{P}
is the unit object for a duality between
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
.

Hence, to show that
\Eis_{P\to Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}
and
\CT_{Q^{-}\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}
are mutually dual, we only need to provide a canonical equivalence

\Eis_{P\to Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id({\mathcal{K}}_{P})\simeq\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\CT_{Q^{-}\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{K}}_{Q})

in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
. This can be constructed as follows. Recall that the maps
\overline{\Delta}^{\mathrm{enh}}_{\leq P}
and the objects
{\mathbf{K}}(P)
are functorial in
P
. Hence we obtain a morphism

\Eis_{P\to Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Eis_{P^{-}\to Q^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{K}}_{P})\to{\mathcal{K}}_{Q},

which induces a morphism

\Eis_{P\to Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id({\mathcal{K}}_{P})\to\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\CT_{Q^{-}\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}({\mathcal{K}}_{Q}).

We only need to show that this morphism is invertible.

When
Q=G
, this is exactly the content of the proof of
[
Che21
, Theorem 5.3.5]
in
[
Che21
, §6.13.2]
. The proof there reduces the claim to
[
Che21
, Goal 6.4.3]
, and then to
[
Che21
, Goal 6.4.17]
. In the general case, we can proceed as in
loc.cit.
and reduce it to
[
Che21
, Goal 6.4.18]
.

Remark 6.5.4
.

It is very possible that the dualities in Theorem
6.5.1
are compatible with all the functorialities of
{\mathcal{I}}(-,-)
in Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
. Up to homotopy coherence, this follows from Corollary
6.5.2
and Remark
6.5.3
. However, one needs more geometric inputs to give a homotopy coherent proof, e.g., one needs to relate
\overline{\Bun}_{G}
to
\overline{\Bun}_{M}
.

Remark 6.5.5
.

It is very possible that the dualities in Theorem
6.5.1
are compatible with the
\Sph_{G,x}
-actions on
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
. This is
not
obvious because there is no
\Sph_{G,x}
-action on
\overline{\Bun}_{G}
.

6.6.
The equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
: the dual Weyl filtration

In this subsection, we introduce the dual Weyl filtration on the functor

\alpha_{P}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\xrightarrow{\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}}\DMod(\Bun_{G})\xrightarrow{\CT_{G\leftarrow R}^{\mathrm{enh}}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\xrightarrow{\iota_{L}^{!}}\DMod(\Bun_{L}).

Via the dualities in Corollary
6.5.2
, the above functor is dual to

\DMod(\Bun_{L})\xrightarrow{\iota_{L,!}^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R^{-})\xrightarrow{\Eis_{R^{-}\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}}\DMod(\Bun_{G})\xrightarrow{\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-}).

In other words, we have

\alpha_{P}^{\vee}\simeq\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}.

Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}.}

By the base-change isomorphism, the functor
\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}
is equivalent to the composition of
14
14

14

Here we do not need to distinguish the
!
-pushforward and the
*
-pushforward along
\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}}\to\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
because it is a disjoint union of proper maps.

\displaystyle\epsilon_{P^{-}}:\DMod(\Bun_{L})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})

with

\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}:\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-}).

By definition, we have

\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}}\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,G\backslash\overline{G/U_{R}^{-}}/L\leftarrow P^{-}\backslash G/R^{-}).

Similarly to Construction
6.4.1
, we can define a filtration on this fiber product. This filtration is still labelled by elements
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
because the definition of the poset
W_{G}/W_{R}
is invariant under the Cartan involution.

Construction 6.6.1
.

For any
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and any representative
w\in W_{G}
of it, consider the closure
\overline{P^{-}wR^{-}}
of
P^{-}wR^{-}
in
G
. Note that it only depends on
\overline{w}
. Define

(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{\leq\overline{w}}:=\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,G\backslash\overline{G/U_{R}^{-}}/L\leftarrow P^{-}\backslash\overline{P^{-}wR^{-}}/R^{-})

Then we obtain a filtration of
\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}}
by the poset
W_{G}/W_{R}
. We also consider

(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{=\overline{w}}:=\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,G\backslash\overline{G/U_{R}^{-}}/L\leftarrow P^{-}\backslash P^{-}wR^{-}/R^{-})

As in Remark
6.4.5
, we have

(22)

(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{=\overline{w}}\simeq\widetilde{\Bun}_{(R^{-})^{w}}^{P^{-}\cap(R^{-})^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}},

where
(R^{-})^{w}:=wR^{-}w^{-1}
, which is also the image of
R^{w}
under the Cartan involution.

Construction 6.6.2
.

As in Construction
6.4.2
and Construction
6.4.4
, we obtain a filtration on
\epsilon_{P^{-}}
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}:\DMod(\Bun_{L})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{\leq\overline{w}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

We also have a similar description for
\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}
. Namely,

•

If
P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
, then

\displaystyle\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}:\DMod(\Bun_{L})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{=\overline{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}),

which by (
22
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{(R^{-})^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{(R^{-})^{w}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{(R^{-})^{w}}^{P^{-}\cap(R^{-})^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

•

If
P\notin\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
, then
\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}\simeq 0
.

Composing with
\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}
, we obtain a filtration on
\alpha_{P}^{\vee}
.

In view of Remark
6.5.3
, the natural transformation (
17
) induces a natural transformation

\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\alpha_{Q}^{\vee}\simeq(\alpha_{Q}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}})^{\vee}\to(\alpha_{P})^{\vee}

which, by construction, is given by

(23)

\displaystyle\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\alpha_{Q}^{\vee}\simeq\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow Q^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}\simeq\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{P^{-}\leftarrow Q^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}\to

\displaystyle\to\CT_{G\leftarrow P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Eis_{R^{-},!}\simeq(\alpha_{P})^{\vee}.

Construction 6.6.3
.

Similarly to Construction
6.4.3
, the natural transformation (
23
) is compatible with the filtrations.

In more detail, consider the map
p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}:\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. By definition

\oblv^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\oblv^{U_{Q}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}.

Hence we have a natural transformation

(24)

\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\to\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}

between functors
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
.

On the other hand, the obvious commutative sqaure

\textstyle{(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{\leq\overline{w}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{\leq\overline{w}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}

induces a natural transformation

(25)

(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}\to\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}.

Combining with the previous paragraph, we obtain the desired natural transformation

(26)

\displaystyle\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{\leq\overline{w}}\simeq\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}\to\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}\to

\displaystyle\to\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{\leq\overline{w}}\simeq(\alpha_{P}^{\vee})^{\leq\overline{w}}

Construction 6.6.4
.

Passing back to the dual side, we obtain the
dual Weyl filtration

\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}
on
\alpha_{P}
, where

\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}:=((\alpha_{P}^{\vee})^{\leq\overline{w}})^{\vee}.

The natural transformation (
26
) induces

(27)

\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\to\alpha_{Q}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}.

Remark 6.6.5
.

We have not checked whether the dual Weyl filtration coincides with the Weyl filtration, i.e.,
\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}\simeq\alpha_{P}^{\leq\overline{w}}
. Probably this can be proven by using the description of the unit object of the duality between
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P^{-})
in
[
Che21
]
.

6.7.
The equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
: the cancellation lemma

In this subsection, we prove the cancellation lemma. The proof is very technical and we believe future techniques can simplify it.

Proposition 6.7.1

(Cancellation lemma for the dual Weyl filtration)
.

For fixed
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and any representative
w
of it, let
Q,P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
with
Q\subseteq P
satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

Q\cap R^{w}=P\cap R^{w}
;

(ii)

U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
, where
U_{Q}
(resp.
U_{R}
) is the unipotent radical of
Q
(resp.
R
),
M_{P}
is the Levi subgroup of
P
and
U_{R}^{w}:=wU_{R}w^{-1}
.

Then (
20
) induces an equivalence

\alpha_{Q}^{\prime=\overline{w}}\circ\CT_{P\leftarrow Q}^{\mathrm{enh}}\simeq\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}

between functors
{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{L})
.

Remark 6.7.2
.

In Corollary
6.8.2
, we will show Condition (ii) actually follows from Condition (i) and the assumption
Q\subseteq P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
.

Proof.

By duality, we need to show that the natural transformation

\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}}\to(\alpha_{P}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}}

is an equivalence.

Using the Cartan involution on
G
, Condition (i) and (ii) are equivalent to

(i-)

Q^{-}\cap(R^{-})^{w}=P^{-}\cap(R^{-})^{w}
;

(ii-)

U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P}\subseteq(U_{R}^{-})^{w}
.

Hence, by (
22
), we have

(\Bun_{G}^{P^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{=\overline{w}}\simeq(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})^{=\overline{w}}.

It follows that (
25
) induces an equivalence

(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}\simeq\epsilon_{P^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}.

It remains to show the natural transformation

\Eis_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}\to\Av_{*}^{U_{P}^{-}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q^{-}\to P^{-}}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\epsilon_{Q^{-}}^{=\overline{w}}

induced by (
24
) is an equivalence. To simplify the notations, we apply the Cartan involution and show that

\Eis_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ\epsilon_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}\to\Av_{*}^{U_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\circ(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}\circ\epsilon_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}

is an equivalence.

Similarly to Remark
6.4.8
, our strategy is to show that the images of the functor
\epsilon_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}
are “
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})
-equivariant” objects, and then show the natural transformation (
24
) sends any such object to an invertible morphism.

For this, we first need to define such “
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})
-equivariant objects”. Consider the functor

(28)

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}).

Definition 6.7.3
.

Define
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})
to be the full subcategory sitting in the following Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{eqn-proof-cancellation-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\mathrm{-pull}}

Remark 6.7.4
.

The essential image of the functor
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})\to\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})
is stable under limits. To see this, we can replace the
*
-pullback functor by the
!
-pullback functor and show the latter has a left adjoint. By the contraction principle (see e.g.
[
DG15
, Appendix C]
), the left adjoint, i.e., the
!
-pushforward functor along
\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}\to\Bun_{M_{Q}}
is indeed well-defined and given by the
!
-pullback functor along
\Bun_{M_{Q}}\to\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}
.

Using the base-change isomorphisms, it is easy to see that
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
.

Then to finish the proof of the cancellation lemma (Porposition
6.7.1
), it is enough to prove the following two lemmas:

Lemma 6.7.5
.

For any arrow
Q\subseteq P
in
\Par^{\mathrm{st}}_{G}
, the natural transformation

\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}}
\scriptstyle{(p_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}})_{!}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Eis_{Q\to P}^{\mathrm{enh}}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}^{U_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)}

is invertible when restricted to
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
.

Lemma 6.7.6
.

Let
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and
Q\subseteq P
be an arrow in
\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
. If
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
, then the functor

\displaystyle\epsilon_{Q}^{=\overline{w}}:\DMod(\Bun_{L})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}).

factors through
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.7.5
.

It suffices to prove the assertion after composing the natural transformation in question with the forgetful functor

\iota_{M_{P}}^{!}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)\to\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
. We have a canonical arrow
\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
, where, abusing notation, we view both
{\mathcal{F}}
and
\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}})
as objects in
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})
.

Now note that the functor
\iota_{M_{P}}^{!}\circ\Av_{*}^{U_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
is equivalent to

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}})

because they have the same left adjoint (Lemma
2.3.7
). Then, unwinding the definitions, the assertion of the lemma boils down to proving that the functor

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}})

sends
\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
to an invertible morphism.

To this end, observe that

\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{P{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{P}\simeq\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}};

thus, using proper base-change and Remark
2.3.18
, the above composition is equivalent to

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{P})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}).

Since
\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{P}
and
\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q/U_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{M_{P}}
are pseudo-proper, this is equivalent to

\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q/U_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}).

Then it suffices to prove that

\theta:\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{P}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q/U_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}})

sends
\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
to an invertible morphism.

Using base-change, it is easy to check that
\theta
sends
\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})^{U_{Q}\cap M_{P}({\mathbb{A}})}
into
{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\subseteq\DMod(\Bun_{M_{P}}^{Q/U_{P}{\mathrm{-gen}}})
. Hence we just need to show that, for any
{\mathcal{K}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
, the morphism

\Maps({\mathcal{K}},\theta(\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}})))\to\Maps({\mathcal{K}},\theta({\mathcal{F}}))

is invertible. As shown in the proof of Proposition-Construction
4.1.5
, the left adjoint
\theta^{L}
of
\theta
sends
{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)
to
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
, and, as such, it is just the functor
\Eis:{\mathcal{I}}(P,Q)\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
. Hence we just need to check that the natural arrow

\Maps(\Eis({\mathcal{K}}),\Av_{*}^{U_{Q}({\mathbb{A}})}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\Maps(\Eis({\mathcal{K}}),{\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism: this is obvious because
\Eis({\mathcal{K}})
is contained in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)
.

∎[Lemma
6.7.5
]

To prove Lemma
6.7.6
, we first need to perform some preliminary simplifications. By definition, we need to show that

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})

factors through the fully faithful pullback functor
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})\to\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})
. We write

\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{Q{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{Q}=:(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}},

and similarly when
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
is replaced by
\Bun_{R^{w}}
. By the base-change isomorphism, the above displayed functor is equivalent to

(29)

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}).

Now we use the defect stratification on
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
to reduce to a similar statement about
\Bun_{R^{w}}
. In other words, we prove the following result.

Lemma 6.7.7
.

For
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, let
Q\subseteq P
be an arrow in
\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
such that
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
. Assume that the functor

(30)

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})

factors through the pullback functor
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})\to\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})
. Then so does the functor (
29
).

Proof.

Recall that
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
has a defect stratification such that the disjoint union of the strata is of the form
\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H
, where
H
is certain Hecke ind-stack for
L^{w}
-torsors. Let

\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H\to\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}

be the locally closed embedding for this stratification. Then the composition

\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H\to\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}\to\Bun_{G}

is given by

\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\Bun_{R^{w}}\to\Bun_{G}.

By the same method as in
[
BG02
, Theorem 6.2.10]
, the image of the composition

\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H)

is contained in the image of the pullback functor
\DMod(H)\to\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H)
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be any object in
\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})
such that its
!
-restriction to
\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H
is an object pulled back from
H
. Then we only need to show the functor

\phi:\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})

sends
{\mathcal{F}}
to an object pulled back from
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
.

We can assume
{\mathcal{F}}
is supported on a single connected component
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w},\lambda}
of
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
. Write this connected component as a union
\cup U_{\alpha}
of open substacks such that each
U_{\alpha}
is the union of
finitely
many defect strata. Then
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\lim_{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha}
is the
*
-extension of
{\mathcal{F}}|_{U_{\alpha}}
.

We claim each
\phi({\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha})
is an object pulled back from
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
. To prove the claim, we can replace
{\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha}
by the
*
-extension of
{\mathcal{F}}|^{!}_{Y}
, where
Y
is some defect stratum of
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
. By definition, such
*
-extension is contained in the image of the functor

\varphi:\DMod(H)\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{L^{w}}}}H)\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}).

By the base-change isomorphism, the composition
\phi\circ\varphi
is equivalent to

\DMod(H)\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{(\ref{eqn-proof-lem-proof-cancellation-3-0})}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}).

Hence the assumption on the functor (
30
) implies the claim.

By the above claim and Remark
6.7.4
, the object
\lim_{\alpha}\phi({\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha})
is pulled back from
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
.
It remains to show
\phi(\lim_{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha})\to\lim_{\alpha}\phi({\mathcal{F}}_{\alpha})
is an isomorphism. Recall that each connected component of
\Bun_{Q}
is quasi-compact over
\Bun_{G}
, hence each connected component of

(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

is quasi-compact over
\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
. Thus, for a fixed such connected component, the inverse images of
U_{\alpha}\subseteq\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{w}}
in it stabilize for large enough
U_{\alpha}
. This makes the desired claim manifest.

∎[Lemma
6.7.7
]

Next, let us prove that the assumption of Lemma
6.7.7
holds true. This will conclude the proof of Lemma
6.7.6
. By definition, we have

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,Q\backslash G/R^{w}\leftarrow{\mathbb{B}}(Q\cap R^{w})),

which, up to non-reduced structures, it is isomorphic to

\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,Q\backslash\overline{QR^{w}}/R^{w}\leftarrow{\mathbb{B}}(Q\cap R^{w})).

For any finite collection
\underline{x}=\big\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\big\}
of closed points of
X
, let

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}\subseteq\mathbf{Maps}_{\mathrm{gen}}(X,Q\backslash\overline{QR^{w}}/R^{w}\leftarrow{\mathbb{B}}(Q\cap R^{w}))

be the open substack classifying those maps
X\to Q\backslash\overline{QR^{w}}/R^{w}
