that send the points
x_{i}
, and therefore a Zariski neighborhood of them, into
{\mathbb{B}}(Q\cap R^{w})\subseteq Q\backslash\overline{QR^{w}}/R^{w}
. By definition, when
\underline{x}
varies, these open substacks form a cover.

The following result says we can replace Lemma
6.7.6
to a similar statement concerning these open substacks.

Lemma 6.7.8
.

Let
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
and
Q\subseteq P
be an arrow in
\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
such that
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
. For any finite collection
\underline{x}=\big\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\big\}
of closed points of
X
, suppose that the functor

(31)

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod((\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}).

factors through the pullback functor
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{Q}})\to\DMod(\Bun_{Q/U_{P}})
. Then so does the functor (
30
).

Proof.

The lemma does
not
follow immediately from Zariski descent and Remark
6.7.4
because

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}

can not be covered by finitely many

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}.

However, this problem is not essential because

(32)

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\to\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}

is quasi-compact when restricted to each connected component of the source.

In more detail, recall
\Bun_{R^{w}}\to\Bun_{G}
is quasi-compact when restricted to each connected component of the source, and
\Bun_{Q}\to\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}
is quasi-compact. Hence the last claim of the above paragraph is true. To prove the lemma, we can replace
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
by any fixed quasi-compact open substack
V\subseteq\Bun_{M_{Q}}
and replace the map (
32
) by

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M_{Q}}}}V\to\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M_{Q}}}}V.

Recall that
\Bun_{Q/U_{P}}
is quasi-compact over
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
. Hence the target of the above map is quasi-compact and so is each connected component of the source. Now each such connected component can be covered by finitely many

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{M_{Q}}}}V,

and we are done by Zariski descent and Remark
6.7.4
.

∎[Lemma
6.7.8
]

Proof of Lemma
6.7.6
.

We just need to show that the assumption of Lemma
6.7.8
is valid. Write

1\to K_{1}\to G_{1}\to H_{1}\to 1,\;\;1\to K_{2}\to G_{2}\to H_{2}\to 1

for the short exact sequences

1\to(Q\cap U_{R}^{w})\to Q\cap R^{w}\to(Q\cap R^{w})/(Q\cap U_{R}^{w})\to 1,\;\;1\to U_{Q}/U_{P}\to Q/U_{P}\to Q/U_{Q}\to 1.

We claim both sequences have canonical splittings. For the second sequence, the splitting is provided by the isomorphism

M_{Q}\to Q\to Q/U_{P}\to Q/U_{Q}.

For the first one, we only need to show that

Q\cap L^{w}\to Q\cap R^{w}\to(Q\cap R^{w})/(Q\cap U_{R}^{w})

is an isomorphism. Note that both
Q
and
L^{w}
contain the Cartan subgroup
T
. Hence, the weight decomposition of
\Lie(G)
implies the above map induces an isomorphism of Lie algebras. On the other hand, choose
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to T
such that the adjoint
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
R^{w}
contracts it onto
L^{w}
. The restricted action on the closed subscheme
Q\cap R^{w}
must contract it onto
Q\cap L^{w}
. In particular,
Q\cap L^{w}
has non-empty intersection with any connected component of
Q\cap R^{w}
. This finishes the proof of the claim.

Write
K_{0}:=U_{Q}\cap M_{P}
. By assumption,
K_{0}\subseteq K_{1}
, and the composition
K_{0}\to G_{1}\to G_{2}
induces an isomorphism
K_{0}\to K_{2}
. Note that
K_{0},K_{1},K_{2}
are unipotent.

Consider the fiber product

\Hecke_{G_{2},\underline{x}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Hecke_{H_{2},\underline{x}}}}\Bun_{H_{2}}

studied in the proof of Lemma
2.4.1
. Informally, it classifies Hecke modifications of
G_{2}
-torsors at
\underline{x}
that induce trivial Hecke modifications of the induced
H_{2}
-torsors. As in the proof of Lemma
2.4.1
, an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{G_{2}})
is pulled back from
\Bun_{H_{2}}
iff the pullbacks of
{\mathcal{F}}
along the two maps

\Bun_{G_{2}}\xleftarrow{h^{l}}\Hecke_{G_{2},\underline{x}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Hecke_{H_{2},\underline{x}}}}\Bun_{H_{2}}\xrightarrow{h^{r}}\Bun_{G_{2}}

are isomorphic.

Now we reformulate the above condition using loop group actions. Consider the
G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-torsor
Z_{2}
on
\Bun_{G_{2}}
that classifies
G_{2}
-torsors equipped with a trivialization on
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}
. The standard re-gluing construction entends the
G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-action on
Z_{2}
to a
G_{2}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})
-action. Note that we have

\DMod(\Bun_{G_{2}})\simeq\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}.

It follows the above condition is equivalent to
{\mathcal{F}}\in\DMod(Z_{2})^{K_{2}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}
.

Similarly, we can define a
G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-torsor
Z_{1}
on
(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}
and extend the
G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-action on
Z_{1}
to a
G_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})
-action. Note that the
K_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})
-action on the source of

Z_{1}\to Z_{1}/G_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})\simeq(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}\to\Bun_{L^{w}}

stabilizes the fibers (because
K_{1}
is contained in the kernel of
Q\cap R^{w}\to L^{w}
). Hence the image of the
!
-pullback functor

\DMod(\Bun_{L^{w}})\to\DMod((\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}})\simeq\DMod(Z_{1})^{G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}

is contained in the full subcategory

\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\subseteq\DMod(Z_{1})^{G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}.

Also note that there is a canonical map
Z_{1}\to Z_{2}
intertwining the
G_{i}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})
-actions and compatible with the map

(\Bun_{R^{w}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{Q})^{Q\cap R^{w}\textnormal{ near }\underline{x}}\to\Bun_{M_{Q}}.

It remains to show the functor

(33)

\DMod(Z_{1})^{G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\simeq\DMod(Z_{1}/G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(Z_{2}/G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\simeq\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}

sends
K_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-equivariant objects to
K_{2}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-equivariant objects. This does
not
follow immediately from
K_{1}\supseteq K_{0}\simeq K_{2}
because
K_{0}
is not a normal subgroup of
G_{1}
hence we cannot define the notion of
K_{0}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-equivariant objects.

Nevertheless, this can still be formally proved as follows.

We first replace the relevant ind-group schemes by group schemes. This step is standard. Namely, write
K_{2}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})\simeq\bigcup_{\alpha}V_{2}^{\alpha}
as union of group schemes stabilized by the adjoint action of
H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
. For example,
V_{2}^{\alpha}
can be chosen to be
hK_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})h^{-1}
for certain central elements
h
in
H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
. We can assume that
V_{2}^{\alpha}
contains
K_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
. For a fixed such
V_{2}:=V_{2}^{\alpha}
, let
V_{0}
be the corresponding subgroup of
K_{0}({\mathcal{K}}_{x})
. Let
V_{1}\subseteq K_{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{\underline{x}})
be a sub-group scheme containing
V_{0}
and stabilized by
H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
. We only need to show that the functor (
33
) sends
V_{1}H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-equivariant objects to
V_{2}H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-equivariant objects.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.7.8
, we replace
\Bun_{M_{Q}}
by a fixed quasi-compact open substack and therefore assume
Z_{i}/G_{i}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
are quasi-compact algebraic stacks. In particular, we have Verdier self-duality on them. Passing to duality, we just need to show that the functor

\displaystyle\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\simeq\DMod(Z_{2}/G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1}/G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\DMod(Z_{1})^{G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}

sends the kernel of

\Av_{*}:\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\to\DMod(Z_{2})^{V_{2}H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}

to
0
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be this kernel. We have a commutative square

\textstyle{\DMod(Z_{1})^{G_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\oblv}
\textstyle{\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}H_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oblv}
\textstyle{\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\textstyle{\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}},}

whose vertical arrows are conservative. Hence we just need to check that

\displaystyle\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\simeq\DMod(Z_{2}/G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1}/K_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}}

sends
{\mathcal{C}}
to
0
. Since
\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\subseteq\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}
, the above functor is equivalent to

\displaystyle\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\simeq\DMod(Z_{2}/G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1}/K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}}))\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}},

which in turn is equivalent to

\displaystyle\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(Z_{2})^{K_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{0}}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{1}}.

Thus, it suffices to prove that

\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(Z_{2})^{K_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{0}}

sends
{\mathcal{C}}
to
0
. Note that the
!
-pullback functor
\DMod(Z_{2})\to\DMod(Z_{1})
is compatible with the
K_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})\simeq K_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})
-actions. By
[
Ras16
, Corollary 2.17.10]
, the above composition is equivalent to

\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(Z_{2})^{K_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{2})^{V_{2}}\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(Z_{1})^{V_{0}}.

Then we are done because the composition of the first two functors is equivalent to

\DMod(Z_{2})^{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}\DMod(Z_{2})^{V_{2}H_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\underline{x}})}\xrightarrow{\oblv}\DMod(Z_{2})^{V_{2}}.

∎[Lemma
6.7.6
]

∎[Proposition
6.7.1
]

6.8.
The equivalence
\Id\to\beta_{G}\circ\beta_{G}^{L}
: finishing the proof

In this subsection, we use the cancellation lemma (Proposition
6.7.1
) to finish the proof.

We first give the following convenient description of
\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
.

Lemma 6.8.1
.

For
P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}
, we have

P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}\;\;\;\text{iff}\;\;\;B\cap M_{P}\subseteq R^{w},

where
B
is the fixed Borel subgroup,
M_{P}
is the Levi subgroup of
P
,
w\in W_{G}
is any representative of
\overline{w}
and
R^{w}:=wRw^{-1}
.

Proof.

By definition of the Bruhat order,
P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
iff
BwR
is closed in
PwR
iff
BR^{w}
is closed in
PR^{w}
iff
U_{P}\backslash BR^{w}
is closed in
U_{P}\backslash PR^{w}
.

Note that
U_{P}\backslash PR^{w}\simeq U_{P}\backslash(P\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(P\cap R^{w})\simeq(M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(P\cap R^{w})
and
U_{P}\backslash BR^{w}\simeq(B\cap M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(B\cap R^{w})
. Hence the above condition is equivalent to the map

(34)

(B\cap M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(B\cap R^{w})\to(M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(P\cap R^{w})

being a closed embedding.

Note that
M_{P}R^{w}=(M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(M_{P}\cap R^{w})\to(M_{P}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}R^{w})/(P\cap R^{w})
is a
U_{P}\cap R^{w}
-torsor. Hence (
34
) is a closed embedding iff so is its base-change along this map. This base-change is
(B\cap M_{P})R^{w}\to M_{P}R^{w}
. It is a closed embedding iff
(B\cap M_{P})R^{w}/R^{w}\to M_{P}R^{w}/R^{w}
is a closed embedding. This map can be rewritten as

(35)

B\cap M_{P}/(B\cap R^{w}\cap M_{P})\to M_{P}/(R^{w}\cap M_{P}).

Note that
B\cap M_{P}
is a Borel subgroup of
M_{P}
. We claim
R^{w}\cap M_{P}
is a parabolic subgroup of
M_{P}
. To see this, first note that
R^{w}
contains the Cartan subgroup
T
. We can choose a homomorphism
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to T
such that the adjoint
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
G
has attractor equal to
R^{w}
. Then
R^{w}\cap M_{P}
is the attractor of the restricted
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
M_{P}
. This proves the claim.

Using the above claim, we see (
35
) is a closed embedding iff
(B\cap M_{P})(R^{w}\cap M_{P})
is closed in
M_{P}
iff
B\cap M_{P}
is contained in
R^{w}\cap M_{P}
iff
B\cap M_{P}
is contained in
R^{w}
.

∎[Lemma
6.8.1
]

Corollary 6.8.2
.

In the statement of Proposition
6.7.1
, Condition (ii) follows from Condition (i). In other words, for
P,Q\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
such that
P\cap R^{w}=Q\cap R^{w}
, we have
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq U_{R}^{w}
.

Proof.

Note that
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq B\cap M_{P}
. Hence by Lemma
6.8.1
,
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}\subseteq R^{w}
.

Since
U_{Q}\cap M_{P}
and
U_{R}^{w}
are connected, we only need to show that
\Lie(U_{Q}\cap M_{P})\subseteq\Lie(U_{R}^{w})
. Since
P,Q,R^{w}
contain the Cartan subgroup
T
, by the weight decomposition of
\Lie(G)
, we just need to show that
\Lie(U_{Q}\cap M_{P})\cap\Lie(L^{w})=0
. Applying the Cartan involution, this amounts to
\Lie(U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P})\cap\Lie(L^{w})=0
.

Note that
\Lie(U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P})\subseteq\Lie(P)
but
\Lie(U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P})\cap\Lie(Q)=0
. Hence, by
P\cap R^{w}=Q\cap R^{w}
, we have
\Lie(U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P})\cap\Lie(R^{w})=0
. In particular,
\Lie(U_{Q}^{-}\cap M_{P})\cap\Lie(L^{w})=0
as desired.

∎[Corollary
6.8.2
]

Then we give a purely combinatorial sufficient condition for the assumption of the cancellation lemma. For convenience, we introduce the following definition:

Definition 6.8.3
.

For any positive simple root
\check{\alpha}
of
G
and standard parabolics
Q\subseteq P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
, we write
P=Q\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle
if the Dynkin diagram of
M_{P}
is obtained from that of
M_{Q}
by adding the vertex corresponding to
\check{\alpha}
.

Lemma 6.8.4
.

If
\overline{w}\neq\overline{w}_{\circ}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, then there exists a negative simple root
-\check{\alpha}
which is not a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
. Moreover, for
Q\subseteq P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}
such that
P=Q\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle
, we have

(1)

P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
iff
Q\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
;

(2)

If
P,Q\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
, then
P\cap R^{w}=Q\cap R^{w}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\Lie(R^{w})
has all negative simple roots as weights. Then
N^{-}\subseteq R^{w}
hence
R^{w}
has transversal intersection with
B^{-}
, which contradicts
\overline{w}\neq\overline{w}_{\circ}
.

Let
-\check{\alpha}
be a negative simple root which is not a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
. Then
\check{\alpha}
must be a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
.

For (1), by Lemma
6.8.1
, we only need to show that
B\cap M_{P}\subseteq R^{w}
iff
B\cap M_{Q}\subseteq R^{w}
. The “only if” part is obvious. The “if” part follows from the above paragraph because
\Lie(B\cap M_{P})
is generated by
\Lie(B\cap M_{Q})
and the weight space of
\check{\alpha}
.

For (2), suppose
P\cap R^{w}\neq Q\cap R^{w}
. Then there exists a negative root
-\check{\lambda}
which is a weight of
\Lie(P\cap R^{w})
but not of
\Lie(Q\cap R^{w})
. Since
P=Q\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle
, we must have
-\check{\lambda}=-n\check{\alpha}-\check{\mu}
, where
n>0
and
\check{\mu}
is a non-negative linear sum of positive simple roots. We can assume there is no root strictly smaller than
\check{\lambda}
satisfying the same assumption.

Note that
\check{\mu}\neq 0
, otherwise
-\check{\alpha}
would be a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
which contradicts our assumption. It follows that
\check{\lambda}
is not simple: let us write
\check{\lambda}=\check{\lambda}_{1}+\check{\lambda}_{2}
for two strictly positive roots
\check{\lambda}_{1},\check{\lambda}_{2}
. Since
-\check{\lambda}
is a weight of
\Lie(P)
, so are
\check{\lambda}_{1}
and
-\check{\lambda}_{1}
.
In other words,
\pm\check{\lambda}_{1}
are both weights of
\Lie(M_{P})
.
Hence, by the assumption
P\in\Par_{G}^{\overline{w}}
and Lemma
6.8.1
,
\check{\lambda}_{1}
is a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
. Consequently,
-\check{\lambda}_{2}=(-\check{\lambda})+\check{\lambda}_{1}
is also a weight of
\Lie(P\cap R^{w})
. By the minimality assumption on
\check{\lambda}
, we see that
-\check{\lambda}_{2}
must be a weight of
\Lie(Q\cap R^{w})
. Write
\check{\lambda}_{2}
as a sum of positive simple roots. Since
P=Q\langle\check{\alpha}\rangle
, this sum has no
\check{\alpha}
term. By symmetry, the sum for
\check{\lambda}_{1}
has no
\check{\alpha}
term either. But
\check{\lambda}=\check{\lambda}_{1}+\check{\lambda}_{2}=n\check{\alpha}+\check{\mu}
contains
\check{\alpha}
terms. A contradiction!

∎[Lemma
6.8.4
]

Now we are ready to finish the proof of Theorem
5.1.4
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1.4
.

Recall our Goal
6.3.1
: we need to show that

(36)

\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})

is invertible, where
\alpha_{P}=\CT_{R,*}\circ\Eis_{P\to G}^{\mathrm{enh}}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{P}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,P)^{G\mathrm{-atemp}}
and
\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{P\leftarrow Q}({\mathcal{M}}_{Q})\simeq{\mathcal{M}}_{P}
for
Q\subseteq P
.

Using the dual Weyl filtration on
\alpha_{P}
(Construction
6.6.4
), we obtain a filtration on the RHS by
W_{G}/W_{R}
, whose
\leq\overline{w}
-term is

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime\leq\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

To conclude, it suffices to prove the following two lemmas.

Lemma 6.8.5
.

We have:

(1)

The morphism (
36
) factors through an equivalence

\iota_{L}^{!}({\mathcal{M}}_{R})\to\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{1}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P});

(2)

For
\overline{w}\in W_{G}/W_{R}
, if
\overline{w}\neq\overline{1},\overline{w}_{\circ}
, then

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq 0.

Lemma 6.8.6
.

For
\overline{w}=\overline{w}_{\circ}
,

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq 0.

We write the above results as two separate lemmas because they are proved using different ingredients. We will prove the former using the cancellation lemma, while prove the latter using the Deligne–Lusztig duality and Theorem
3.0.2
.

Proof of Lemma
6.8.5
.

In this proof, we do not need the assumption that
{\mathcal{M}}_{P}
is
G
-anti-tempered.

We assume
\overline{w}\neq\overline{w}_{\circ}
. Let
-\check{\alpha}
be as in Lemma
6.8.4
, i.e., a negative simple root which is not a weight of
\Lie(R^{w})
. Let
\Par^{-\check{\alpha}\notin}_{G}\subseteq\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}
be the sub-poset containing those
Q
for which
-\check{\alpha}
is not a weight of
\Lie(Q)
. Then there is a retraction

\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}\to\Par^{-\check{\alpha}\notin}_{G}

sending both
Q\langle\alpha\rangle
and
Q
to
Q
.

We claim that the functor

\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}\to\DMod(\Bun_{L}),\;P\mapsto\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})

factors through the above retraction. As we now explain, this follows easily from the cancellation lemma. We just need to show that the arrow
Q\to Q\langle\alpha\rangle=:P
induces an isomorphism

\alpha_{Q}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{Q})\to\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

Suppose that
Q\notin\Par_{G}^{\underline{w}}
: then
P\notin\Par_{G}^{\underline{w}}
by Lemma
6.8.4
(1), which implies that the source and the target are both
0
(see Construction
6.6.2
). Now suppose that
Q\in\Par_{G}^{=\underline{w}}
: then Lemma
6.8.4
(1) implies that
P\in\Par_{G}^{=\underline{w}}
, too. By Corollary
6.8.2
and Lemma
6.8.4
(2), the assumptions of the cancellation lemma (Proposition
6.7.1
) are satisfied. Hence the desired morphism is an isomorphism.

By the claim, we have

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par^{-\check{\alpha}\notin}_{G}}\,}\alpha_{Q}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{Q})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

Note that
\Par^{-\check{\alpha}\notin}_{G}
has a final object: the maximal standard parabolic subgroup
S
such that the Dynkin diagram of
M_{S}
is obtained from that of
G
by removing the vertex corresponding to
\check{\alpha}
. Consequently,

\alpha_{S}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{S})\simeq\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P}).

When
\underline{w}\neq\underline{1}
, we claim
S\notin\Par_{G}^{=\overline{w}}
.
Of course, this implies (2) because in that case
\alpha_{S}^{\prime=\overline{w}}\simeq 0
(see Construction
6.6.2
). To prove the claim, by Lemma
6.8.1
, we only need to show that
B\cap M_{S}
is not contained in
R^{w}
. Suppose the contrary, then
\Lie(R^{w})
contains all the positive simple roots of
M_{S}
. On the other hand, by definition,
\Lie(R^{w})
contains
\check{\alpha}
. Hence
R^{w}
is a standard parabolic subgroup. Then
B\cap M_{S}\subseteq R^{w}
implies
R^{w}=S
because both
R^{w}
and
S
are maximal proper standard parabolic subgroups, and neither of them contains
-\check{\alpha}
as a weight. But this forces
\underline{w}=\underline{1}
, because
S
is standard. This finishes the proof of (2).

When
\underline{w}=\underline{1}
, we have
S=R
. Hence to prove (1), we need to show the natural transformation
\iota_{L}^{!}\to\alpha_{R}
factors through an equivalence
\iota_{L}^{!}\simeq\alpha_{R}^{\prime=\overline{1}}
.

Recall (see Construction
6.6.2
) that
(\alpha_{R}^{\vee})^{=\overline{1}}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{R^{-}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{R^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,R^{-}),

which by definition is equivalent to
\iota_{L,!}^{-}:\DMod(\Bun_{L})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,R^{-})
. Passing to duality, we see
\alpha_{R}^{\prime=\overline{1}}\simeq\iota_{L}^{!}:{\mathcal{I}}(G,R)\to\DMod(\Bun_{L})
(see Corollary
6.5.2
). It follows from the construction that
\iota_{L}^{!}\to\alpha_{R}
indeed factors through this equivalence. This finishes the proof of (1).

∎[Lemma
6.8.5
]

It remains to prove Lemma
6.8.6
. We first need some preparations. Let
S:=(R^{-})^{w_{\circ}}:=w_{\circ}R^{-}w_{\circ}^{-1}
. Note that
S
is a maximal standard (proper) parabolic subgroup of
G
. Let
-\check{\beta}
be the unique negative simple root which is not a weight of
\Lie(S)
.

Recall
\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\subseteq\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}
is the full subcategory of standard parabolic subgroups
P
contained in
S
. We claim

\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}=\Par_{G}^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}.

By Lemma
6.8.1
, we only need to show that
B\cap M_{P}\subseteq S^{-}
iff
P\subseteq S
, but this is obvious.

Let
[1]
be the 1-simplex viewed as a category, i.e., it contains two objects
0
and
1
, and a unique non-invertible arrow
0\to 1
. Then we have an equivalence

(37)

(\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{S\}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}[1])\bigsqcup_{\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{S\}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}\{0\}}\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\to\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{G\}

which sends

•

(Q,0)\in(\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{S\}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}[1])
to
Q
;

•

(Q,1)\in(\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\setminus\{S\}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}[1])
to
Q\langle\check{\beta}\rangle
;

•

P\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}
to
P
.

By Construction
6.6.2
, if
P\notin\Par_{G}^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}=\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}
, then
\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}\simeq 0
. Hence the equivalence (
37
) implies

\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{P\in\Par_{G}^{\mathrm{st}},P\neq G}\,}\alpha_{P}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}({\mathcal{M}}_{P})\simeq\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}},Q\neq S}\,}\alpha_{Q}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}({\mathcal{M}}_{Q})\to\alpha_{S}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}({\mathcal{M}}_{S}))

By assumption, we have
{\mathcal{M}}_{Q}\simeq\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S\leftarrow Q}({\mathcal{M}}_{S})
. Hence it remains to prove that

(38)

\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}},Q\neq S}\,}\alpha_{Q}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}}\circ\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S\leftarrow Q}\to\alpha_{S}^{\prime=\overline{w}_{\circ}})

sends
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)^{G\mathrm{-atemp}}
to
0
.

By Construction
6.6.4
, the functor (
38
) is the dual functor of

(39)

\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}},Q\neq S}\,}\Eis^{\mathrm{enh}}_{Q^{-}\to S^{-}}\circ(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}\to(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}),

where each
(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}
is equivalent to (see Construction
6.6.2
)

(40)

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{S})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S}^{Q^{-}\cap S{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q^{-}).

We claim the following diagram is Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Bun}_{S}^{Q^{-}\cap S{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Bun}_{S}^{S^{-}\cap S{\mathrm{-gen}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

The claim follows from

{\mathbb{B}}(Q^{-}\cap S)\simeq{\mathbb{B}}(S^{-}\cap S)\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{{\mathbb{B}}S^{-}}}{\mathbb{B}}Q^{-},

which in turn is a consequence of
(S^{-}\cap S)Q^{-}=S^{-}
.

Applying the base-change isomorphism to this diagram, we see that
(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\DMod(\Bun_{L})\simeq\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{S})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S})\to

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q^{-}).

By Definition
2.3.16
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!\mathrm{-pull}}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\textstyle{\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{Q^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Av_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S^{-}\leftarrow Q^{-}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q^{-}).}

We obtain

(\alpha_{Q}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}\simeq\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S^{-}\leftarrow Q^{-}}\circ(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}

and can thus rewrite (
39
) as

\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}},Q\neq S}\,}\Eis^{\mathrm{enh}}_{Q^{-}\to S^{-}}\circ\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S^{-}\leftarrow Q^{-}}\to\Id)\circ(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}.

Now consider the endo-functor of
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})
defined by

(41)

\mathrm{DL}_{S^{-}}:=\coFib(\mathbin{\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits_{Q\in\Par_{S}^{\mathrm{st}},Q\neq S}\,}\Eis^{\mathrm{enh}}_{Q^{-}\to S^{-}}\circ\CT^{\mathrm{enh}}_{S^{-}\leftarrow Q^{-}}\to\Id).

We have reduced Lemma
6.8.6
(and therefore the entire proof) to the following goal.

Goal 6.8.7
.

For any
{\mathcal{F}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{L})
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)^{G\mathrm{-atemp}}
, we have

\langle\mathrm{DL}_{S^{-}}\circ(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{M}}\rangle\simeq 0,

where
\langle-,-\rangle
is the pairing functor between
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})
and
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)
.

We first deduce this from the following lemmas.

Lemma 6.8.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})^{\iota_{*}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})
be the full subcategory generated under colimits by the image of
\iota_{M_{S},*}^{-}:\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})\to{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})
(see Definition
2.3.13
). Then the image of
(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}
is contained in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})^{\iota_{*}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
.

Lemma 6.8.9
.

The image of the functor

{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{DL}_{S}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)\xrightarrow{\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})

is contained in
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})^{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.8.6
.

By Goal
6.8.7
and Lemma
6.8.8
, it suffices to prove that

\langle\mathrm{DL}_{S^{-}}\circ\iota_{M_{S},*}^{-}({\mathcal{K}}),{\mathcal{M}}\rangle\simeq 0,

for any
{\mathcal{K}}\in\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)^{G\mathrm{-atemp}}
. By Corollary
6.5.2
and Remark
6.5.3
, the functor
\mathrm{DL}_{S^{-}}\circ\iota_{M_{S},*}^{-}
is dual to
\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}
. Hence we need to prove that

\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}({\mathcal{M}})\simeq 0.

By Lemma
6.8.9
,

\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}({\mathcal{M}})\in\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})^{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}

and therefore is
M_{S}
-tempered (
[
Ber21b
, Theorem B]
).

On the other hand, the functor
\mathrm{DL}_{S}
is
\Sph_{G,x}
-linear, hence
\mathrm{DL}_{S}({\mathcal{M}})
is
G
-anti-tempered because so is
{\mathcal{M}}
. Theorem
3.0.2
(4) implies that
\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}({\mathcal{M}})
is also
M_{S}
-anti-tempered.

Combining the above two paragraphs, we see
\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}({\mathcal{M}})\simeq 0
as desired.

∎[Lemma
6.8.6
]

It remains to prove Lemma
6.8.8
and Lemma
6.8.9
.

Proof of Lemma
6.8.8
.

By (
40
), the functor
(\alpha_{S}^{\vee})^{=\overline{w}_{\circ}}
factors through

\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-}).

We claim that the image of above functor already lands in
{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})^{\iota_{*}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. Let
\lambda\in\Lambda_{G,S}
and consider the corresponding connected component
\widetilde{\Bun}_{S,\lambda}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. By Corollary
2.3.14
, we only need to show that

\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S,\lambda}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{\Av_{*}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S^{-})\xrightarrow{(\iota_{M_{S}}^{-,\mu})^{!}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S},\mu})

is
0
unless
\mu\geq\lambda
. Here we flip the sign of the inequality in the statement of Corollary
2.3.14
because we apply it to
S^{-}
.

By definition, the above composition is given by

\DMod(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S,\lambda}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}})\xrightarrow{!\mathrm{-pull}}\DMod(\Bun_{S^{-},\mu})\xrightarrow{*\mathrm{-push}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S},\mu}).

By the base-change isomorphism, it suffices to check that

\widetilde{\Bun}_{S,\lambda}^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}^{S^{-}{\mathrm{-gen}}}}}\Bun_{S^{-},\mu}=\emptyset

unless
\mu\geq\lambda
. Then we are done: this fiber product is just

(\widetilde{\Bun}_{S,\lambda}\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits_{\Bun_{G}}}\Bun_{S^{-},\mu})^{M_{S}{\mathrm{-gen}}},

i.e. the parabolic Zastava space defined in
[
BFGM02
, §2]
, and the desired inequality was proved there.

∎[Lemma
6.8.8
]

Proof of Lemma
6.8.9
.

We will show that the image of

(42)

\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})\xrightarrow{\iota_{M_{S},!}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{DL}_{S}}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)\xrightarrow{\iota_{M_{S}}^{*}}\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})

is contained in
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})^{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}
.

Consider the functor
\mathrm{DL}_{M_{S}}:\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})\to\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})
in Theorem
6.1.4
(applied to the reductive group
M_{S}
). Using notations in §
4.1
, it is a certain colimit of functors

{\mathcal{I}}(S,S)\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{I}}(S,Q)\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{I}}(S,S).

On the other hand, by definition (see (
41
), the functor
\mathrm{DL}_{S}
is a similar colimit of functors

{\mathcal{I}}(G,S)\xrightarrow{\CT}{\mathcal{I}}(G,Q)\xrightarrow{\Eis}{\mathcal{I}}(G,S).

By the base-change isomorphism for
{\mathcal{I}}(-,-)
(Lemma
4.5.1
), it is easy to see that

\iota_{M_{S}}^{!}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{S}\simeq\mathrm{DL}_{M_{S}}\circ\iota_{M_{S}}^{!}.

Passing to dualities (Corollary
6.5.2
and Remark
6.5.3
), we obtain that

\mathrm{DL}_{S}\circ\iota_{M_{S},!}\simeq\iota_{M_{S},!}\circ\mathrm{DL}_{M_{S}}.

Hence the functor (
42
) is equivalent to
\mathrm{DL}_{M_{S}}
. By Theorem
6.1.4
, it suffices to show that the image of the functor
\PsId_{M_{S},*}
is contained in
\DMod(\Bun_{M_{S}})^{*{\mathrm{-gen}}}
. But this is known, see
[
DG13
, §4.4.3]
.

∎[Lemma
6.8.9
]

∎[Theorem
5.1.4
]

Appendix A
Lax
\Sph_{G}
-linear functors are strict

In this appendix, we prove the following result:

Proposition A.1.1
.

Any
continuous
(left or right)-lax
\Sph_{G}
-linear is strictly
\Sph_{G}
-linear.

Definition A.1.2
.

Following
[
GR19
, Chapter 1, Definition 9.1.2]
, a stable presentable monoidal category
{\mathcal{A}}
is
rigid
if the following conditions hold:

(i)

The right adjoint
m^{R}:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{A}}
of the multiplication functor
m
is continuous;

(ii)

The functor
m^{R}
, which a priori is right-lax
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{rev}})
-linear, is strictly linear;

(iii)

The unit object
1_{\mathcal{A}}
is compact.

Proposition A.1.3

(​​
[
GR19
, Chaper 1, Lemma 9.3.6]
)
.

For a presentable monoidal category
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfying Condition (i) and (ii) in Definition
A.1.2
, any continuous lax
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear functor is strictly linear
15
15

15

Warning: the proof in
loc.cit.
contains
lots
of typos.
.

∎[Proposition
A.1.3
]

Remark A.1.4
.

In fact,
[
GR19
]
stated the above results for rigid monoidal categories. However, the proofs there did not use Condition (iii), nor the stable assumption on
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Remark A.1.5
.

\Sph_{G}
is
not
rigid because its unit object is not compact. Indeed, the unit object is the pushforward of
\omega
along
{\mathbb{B}}G({\mathcal{O}})\to G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})
and
\omega\in\DMod({\mathbb{B}}G({\mathcal{O}}))\simeq\DMod({\mathbb{B}}G)
is not compact (see
[
DG13
, §7.1.4]
).

Nevertheless, we have:

Lemma A.1.6
.

The monoidal category
\Sph_{G}
satisfies Condition (i) and (ii) in Definition
A.1.2
.

Proof.

Recall the multiplication functor
m
is given by
!
-pull-
*
-push along the following correspondence

G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})\xleftarrow{u}G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits^{G({\mathcal{O}})}}G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})\xrightarrow{v}G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})\mathbin{\mathop{\times}\displaylimits}G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}),

where
u
is ind-proper and
v
is pro-smooth and in particular placid (see
[
Ras15
, §4.10]
). It follows that
m\simeq u_{*}\circ v^{!}\simeq u_{!}\circ v^{!}
has a continuous right adjoint
m^{R}\simeq v_{*,\mathrm{ren}}\circ u^{!}
, where
v_{*,\mathrm{ren}}
is the renormalized pushforward functor defined in
loc.cit.
. Hence we verified Condition (i). Also, Condition (ii) follows from the base-change isomorphisms between such renormalized functors and usual
!
-pullback functors (see
[
Ras15
, Proposition. 4.11.1]
).

∎[Lemma
A.1.6
]

Proof of Proposition
A.1.1
.

Now Proposition
A.1.1
follows from Lemma
A.1.6
and Proposition
A.1.3
.

∎[Proposition
A.1.1
]

We also need the following technical lemma.

Lemma A.1.7

(​​
[
GR19
, Chapter 1, Lemma 9.3.2]
.)
.

For a presentable monoidal category
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfying Condition (i) and (ii) in Definition
A.1.2
and any left
{\mathcal{A}}
-module
{\mathcal{M}}
, the action functor
a:{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{M}}
has a continuous right adjoint
a^{R}
, which is canonically isomorphic to

{\mathcal{M}}\xrightarrow{1_{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}\xrightarrow{m^{R}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}\Id}{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}\xrightarrow{\Id\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}a}{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}.

∎[Lemma
A.1.7
]
